3
Summary
External environment
World economy was marked in 2015 by a metamorphosis of
trends. A signiﬁcant slowdown in China’s economy is to be ﬁrst
mentioned, which generated turbulences on the foreign exchange
and stock exchange market. The economic crisis in the Russian
Federation and Brazil hastened the decline of emerging and
developing countries. The most important event in the monetary
and foreign exchange ﬁeld was the decision of the Federal
Reserve System of 16 December 2015 to increase the interest
rates by 0.25 percentage points, the decision itself and its
anticipation
served
as
the
main
cause
of
the
U.S.
dollar
appreciation during the reporting period. At the same time, the
U.S. dollar appreciation and the weak world demand determined
the decrease in stock exchange quotations for raw materials, from
food products to heavy metals. The downward trend in oil price
was more pronounced in 2015, while the global oil surplus
increased. The euro area economy evolved stable and the ECB
quantitative
easing
programme
was
relatively
efﬁcient
to
counteract the disinﬂationary pressures. However, in the second
half of 2015, the refugee crisis in Europe created a new wave of
social problems with a negative impact on the European Union
economy. At the same time, the crisis in the Russian Federation
and the depreciation of the Russian ruble was the epicentre of the
crisis of the Commonwealth of Independent States.
Economic developments
In the reporting year, the economy of the Republic of Moldova
recorded a decline of 0.5 percent, this annual dynamics being by
5.1 percentage points lower than in 2014.
The negative
development of the economic activity was mainly determined by
the drought in 2015, which reduced signiﬁcantly the performance
of agricultural sector and by the deterioration of the regional
economic climate and the persistence of an internal economic and
political uncertainty generated the decrease in domestic demand
from population and investments contraction. In this regard, ﬁnal
consumption of households and the ﬁxed capital gross formation
decreased by 2.3 and 1.2 percent, respectively, compared to
2014.
The negative impact of changes in inventories to GDP
dynamics was more pronounced. Similar to the previous years,
the government consumption has not signiﬁcantly inﬂuenced the
dynamics of economic activity, recording a decrease of 0.4
percent. The negative impact of the aforementioned factors was
partially mitigated by the depreciation of the national currency
against the U.S. dollar, which compensated for the contraction of
foreign
currency
inﬂows
and
that
also
improved
the
competitiveness of the domestic products.


4
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
Consequently,
the decrease in domestic demand and the
depreciation of national currency led to a signiﬁcant decrease in
imports (4.3 percent). At the same time, the exports of goods and
services recorded an increase of 2.3 percent in 2015.
The gross value added in agriculture recorded a decline of 13.4
percent due to unfavourable meteorological conditions, after two
years where it supported the economic activity.
A negative
evolution, which generated a less signiﬁcant impact in 2015, was
recorded
in
trade.
The
negative
contribution
of
the
aforementioned components was mitigated by the positive
dynamics of the ﬁnancial, industrial and communications sector.
Inﬂation
In 2015, the annual inﬂation rate was 9.7 percent, by 4.6
percentage points more compared to 2014.
During 2015, the
annual inﬂation rate had a pronounced upward trajectory. This
increased from 4.7 percent in January to 7.1 percent in March
under
the
pressure
of
the
national
currency
depreciation,
exceeding thus the upper limit of the range of variation of ±1.5
percentage points from 5.0 percent inﬂation target stipulated in
the Medium-term monetary policy strategy. Subsequently, the
annual inﬂation rate increased gradually to 8.6 percent in July
2015, being inﬂuenced by the depreciation effects and the
unfavourable
agro-meteorological
conditions
that
exerted
pressures on food prices. The increase in electricity and gas tariffs
determined a pronounced inﬂation acceleration to 12.2 percent in
August, while at the end of 2015 the inﬂation rate was 13.6
percent. During the year, the existing inﬂationary pressures were
partially
mitigated
by
the
modest
domestic
demand,
the
embargoes on certain categories of products and the downward
trend of food and oil prices internationally.
Monetary policy
During 2015, there were held thirteen meetings of the Executive
Board of the National Bank of Moldova on monetary policy
decisions. Following the assessment of the balance of internal and
external risks to which the economy of the Republic of Moldova
could be subject and the inﬂation outlook in the short and
medium-term, there were issued seven decisions to maintain and
six decisions to increase the base rate applied on main short-term
monetary policy operations from the level of 6.5 percent to 19.5
percent annually and the required reserves ratio attracted in MDL
and non-convertible currency from the level of 14.0 percent to
35.0 percent of the base.
The growth rates of monetary indicators decreased in 2015. Thus,
the dynamic of monetary aggregates was negative, the recorded
values being below the growth level recorded in the previous
years, representing in annual terms -9.2 percent for M2 (by 5.0
percentage points lower than in 2014) and -3.0 percent for M3 (by
8.3 percentage points lower than in 2014).


Summary
5
During the reporting period, the average annual interest rate on
new loans granted in national currency increased by 3.47
percentage points, while the average annual interest rate on new
loans granted in foreign currency decreased by 0.98 percentage
points compared to 2014, recording values of 14.06 percent in
national currency and 7.01 percent in foreign currency.
The
average interest rate on deposits in MDL accounted for 12.10
percent, increasing by 6.38 percentage points compared to 2014,
while the interest rate on placements in foreign currency
constituted
on
average
2.56
percent,
decreasing
by
1.63
percentage points compared to 2014.
Banking system
Given the systemic ﬁnancial crisis situation, the need to ensure
ﬁnancial stability and protect the interests of depositors, within
the meeting of 5 August 2015 of the National Committee for
Financial Stability it has decided to withdraw the license of Banca
de Economii S.A., BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. „UNIBANK”
S.A. At the same time, in order to avoid the disturbance of the
ﬁnancial stability of the banking system or the activity of public
institutions, the Government of the Republic of Moldova, the
National Bank of Moldova and some authorities of central public
administration and public institutions concluded a Memorandum
of understanding "On the measures to be taken to withdraw the
licences of Banca de Economii S.A., BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and
B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A. and the time-limits for their achievement".
In accordance with the provisions of the Memorandum, each
authority performed a number of actions relating to the transfer
of the treasury accounts and other accounts of the state budget,
social payments and other compensations, and the transfer of the
accounts of individuals and legal entities from the aforementioned
banks to other banks. Thus, Banca de Economii S.A., BC „BANCA
SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A., with the assistance of the
National Bank, transferred the accounts of individuals and legal
entities to other banks on 12-15 October 2015. As of 30 September
2015, the funds of depositors and economic agents transferred in
other commercial banks recorded the level of MDL 737.3 million or
44.5 percent of the total deposits of individuals and legal entities.
The Executive Board of the National Bank of Moldova withdrew
on 16 October 2015 the licence for ﬁnancial activity of Banca de
Economii S.A., BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A.
as a result of their insolvency and other violations of the legislation
in force and appointed liquidators for the forced liquidation of the
banks.
It should be mentioned that since the withdrawal of the licences
of Banca de Economii S.A., BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C.
„UNIBANK” S.A. until 31 December 2015, these banks collected
ﬁnancial means (from loans payments, sale of pledge, sale of
assets, other receipts) in the total amount of MDL 326.5 million,
including: Banca de Economii S.A. – MDL 153.3 million; BC „BANCA
SOCIAL˘A” S.A. – MDL 96.1 million; B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A. – MDL 77.1
million. At the same time, from the withdrawal of licences to 31
December 2015, these banks have reimbursed the debts related


6
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
to emergency loans granted before the appointment of liquidators
in the total amount of MDL 441.4 million, of which: Banca de
Economii S.A. – MDL 74.8 million; BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. – MDL
121.8 million; B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A. – MDL 244.7 million.
As at 31 December 2015, the banking sector of the Republic
of Moldova included 11 banks licensed by the National Bank of
Moldova, of which 4 are branches of foreign banks and ﬁnancial
groups.
In accordance with the provisions of the Law on Financial
Institutions, the National Bank of Moldova established a special
supervision procedure for three months to three banks (BC
„Moldindconbank”
S.A.,
B.C.
„VICTORIABANK”
S.A.
and
BC
„MOLDOVA - AGROINDBANK” S.A., which hold in total 66.7 percent
of the banking sector assets) of those 11 banks licensed by the
NBM, following the detection of issues related to non-transparent
shareholder structure, engaging in high risk lending operations. At
the same time, the National Bank of Moldova has prescribed by
this decision a diagnostic study to these banks, which shall be
conducted by a company specialized in this ﬁeld.
It should be mentioned that on 12 September 2015 and
subsequently on 14 December 2015, National Bank maintained
the special supervision procedure for another three months, as a
result
of
the
unsolved
issues
related
to
transparency
of
shareholder structure and engaging in high risk operations.
Tier I on the banking system reached the level of MDL 8 995.7
million, increasing by MDL 1 384.9 million (18.2 percent) compared
to the end of 2014.
Tier I capital increase was driven mainly by the proﬁt in the amount
of MDL 1 442.7 million obtained during 2015. At the same time,
during the reporting period, four banks have distributed dividends
in total amount of MDL 164.0 million.
The average of risk-weighted capital adequacy indicator of the
sector recorded the value of 26.2 percent, increasing by 4.5
percentage points compared to the end of 2014.
As of 31
December 2015, all banks have complied with the risk-weighted
capital adequacy indicator (the limit for each bank is ≥16.0
percent).
The total assets of the sector were MDL 69 095.6 million, increasing
by MDL 9 117.0 million (15.2 percent) compared with the end of
2014.
The share of non-performing loans in total loans increased by 1.5
percentage points compared to the end of 2014, amounting to 9.9
percent as at 31 December 2015. The share of net non-performing
loans in total regulatory capital increased by 3.4 percentage points,
amounting to 15.0 percent as at 31 December 2015.
In 2015, return on assets and equity of licensed banks recorded
the value of 2.1 and 12.8 percent, respectively, increasing by 0.5
and 3.5 percentage points, respectively, compared to 2014.
Net interest margin was 5.5 percent as at 31 December 2015, by
0.8 percentage points higher than at the end of 2014.
Long-term liquidity ratio of the banking system (principle I)
accounted for 0.7 (the limit for each bank is ≤1). Current liquidity
per sector (principle II) accounted for 41.5 percent (limit for each
bank is ≥20.0 percent), increasing by 8.6 percentage points
during 2015. Banks complied with both indicators.

Chapter 1
World economy
In 2015, the world economy was marked by a metamorphosis of
trends. A signiﬁcant slowdown in the China’s economy is to be
ﬁrst mentioned, which generated turbulences in the foreign
exchange and stock exchange market.
The economic crisis in
the Russian Federation and Brazil hastened the decline of
emerging and developing economies. The most important event
in the monetary and foreign exchange ﬁeld was the decision of
the Federal Reserve System of 16 December 2015 to increase
the interest rates by 0.25 percentage points, the decision itself
and its anticipation served as the main cause of the U.S. dollar
appreciation during the reporting period. At the same time, the
U.S. dollar appreciation and the weak world demand determined
the decrease in stock exchange quotations for raw materials,
from food products to heavy metals. The downward trend in oil
price was more pronounced in 2015, while the global oil surplus
increased. The euro area economy remained stable and the ECB
quantitative
easing
programme
was
relatively
efﬁcient
to
counteract the disinﬂationary pressures. However, in the second
half of 2015, the refugee crisis in Europe created a new wave of
social problems with a negative impact on the European Union
economy. At the same time, the crisis in the Russian Federation
and the depreciation of the Russian ruble was the epicentre of
the crisis of the Commonwealth of Independent States.
According to the IMF data, the world economy increased in 2015
on average by 3.1 percent compared to 3.4 percent in 2014. As
a result of the recovery in the most of advanced economies, the
average growth rate was 1.9 percent compared to 1.8 percent
in 2014. Chart 1.1 shows that among the advanced economies,
the GDP of the USA recorded a similar growth rate as in 2014,
amounting to 2.4 percent, while the GDP growth rate of the United
Kingdom decreased from 2.9 to 2.2 percent, the other economies

registering increases. At the same time, the rate of economic
activity in emerging and developing countries slowed from the
average of 4.6 percent in 2014 to 4.0 percent in 2015.
The
economy of China increased in 2015 by 6.9 percent, recording
the lowest growth rate over the last quarter of a century. In 2015,
the economy of the Russian Federation decreased by 3.7 percent,
the economy of Ukraine – by 9.9 percent, while the economy of the
Republic of Moldova – by 0.5 percent. Therefore, the economies of
the CIS countries contracted in 2015 on average by 2.8 percent
compared to the increase of 1.0 percent in 2014 (Chart 1.1).
In the reporting year, the foreign exchange markets have been
marked by two major events. The ﬁrst was the decision of the ECB
to initiate a quantitative easing programme, which supposes the
purchase of assets in the amount of EUR 60 billion monthly, in
March 2015 - September 2016 (subsequently, in March 2016,
along with the decrease of base rate to 0.0 percent, the ECB

increased until July 2017 the purchase of assets to EUR 80 billion
monthly). Another event was the decision of the Federal Reserve
System (Fed) of 16 December 2015 to increase the interest rates
from 0-0.25 percent to 0.25-0.50 percent. The increase of interest

Overall, in 2015, the European single currency depreciated
against the U.S. dollar on average by 16.5 percent. Swiss franc
depreciated on average by 5.2 percent against the U.S. dollar and
appreciated by 12.1 percent against the European single currency.

Another important event, which marked the foreign exchange
markets, was the decision of the Swiss National Bank to annul the
threshold of 1.20 for CHF/EUR, which determined the signiﬁcant
appreciation of the Swiss franc. Pound sterling appreciated on
average by 9.9 percent against the EUR and depreciated on
average by 7.7 percent against the USD. Intensiﬁcation of
incentive monetary policies in Japan mitigated the appreciation of
the Japanese yen against the European single currency and
accentuated the depreciation against the U.S. dollar. Japanese
yen appreciated on average by 4.3 percent against the EUR and
depreciated on average by 14.3 percent against the USD. At the
end of 2015, Japanese yen appreciated signiﬁcantly, thus the ratio
between
December
2015
and
December
2014
shows
an
appreciation of 10.0 percent of the Japanese yen against the EUR
and a depreciation of about 2.1 percent against the USD (Table
1.1).
Trends in international foreign exchange markets inﬂuenced also
the evolution of the regional currencies. Thus, worldwide, the
Ukrainian hryvnia recorded the highest depreciation against the
EUR on average by 54.2 percent and against the USD by 83.8
percent.
The decrease in oil prices accentuated more the
depreciation of the Russian ruble, which depreciated during 2015
by 33.5 percent against the EUR and by 58.8 percent against the
USD. Romanian leu maintained on average the same level against
the European single currency but depreciated on average by 19.6
percent against the U.S. dollar, the average value in December
2015 was by 14.4 percent lower to that of December 2014. For
comparison, Moldovan leu depreciated in 2015 on average by
12.2 percent against the EUR and by 34.0 percent against the
USD (Table 1.1).
Global
prices
for
raw
materials
decreased
for
the
fourth
consecutive year, but being more pronounced compared to the
previous years.
Thus, international food prices decreased on
average by 17.1 percent, international metal prices – by 23.1
percent, while those for energy resources – by 44.8 percent. In
2015, global prices for raw materials decreased on average by
35.3 percent.

Chapter 1. World economy
9
Negative trends were determined by the USD appreciation, the
global production surplus and weak global demand (Chart 1.3).
In 2015, the average price for Urals crude oil recorded a value of
USD 51.3 per barrel, which represents a decrease of 47.5 percent
compared to 2014. The decrease in oil prices during the last two
years has also determined the decrease in the import prices for
natural gas. Thus, according to ANRE data, the average import

price for natural gas amounted to USD 244.4 per 1000 m3, by

Since the end of 2013, the global oil surplus constituted the main
factor of the decrease in oil prices. Thus, in 2015, the oil supply
exceeded the demand on average by 2.0 million of barrels per day
(Chart 1.5).
However, futures oil quotations reached in 2015
minimum levels, due to the OPEC decision to increase the
production quota.
At the same time, the USD appreciation
generated by the Fed decision to increase the interest rates has
inﬂuenced the decrease in oil prices.
Another factor, which
inﬂuenced the decrease in oil prices, was the slowdown in the
emerging economies growth that determined the low oil demand.
In 2015, the economy of the Unites States of America increased
by 2.4 percent, similar to 2014, manifested by the improvement
of labour market, higher consumption and through low oil price
advantages. In 2015, industrial production increased on average
by 1.4 percent.
At the same time, the unemployment rate
decreased from 5.7 percent in January 2015 to 5.0 percent in
December 2015.
Amid low prices for energy resources, the
disinﬂationary pressures represented an impediment for the Fed
to increase further the interest rates.
Thus, in 2015, the USA
consumer prices stagnated on average (0.1 percent), while in
December recorded an annual inﬂation of 0.7 percent.
In the reporting year, the euro area economy increased by 1.6
percent, the industrial production increased on average by 1.4
percent, while the unemployment rate decreased to 10.4 percent
in December 2015, overall, the unemployment rate recorded an
annual average of 10.9 percent. Amid the depreciation of the
European single currency, the exports in euro area increased on
average by 5.3 percent, while the imports by 2.3 percent, due to
the consumer’s monetary stimulus. The disinﬂationary pressures
were relatively pronounced in the euro area but given to the ECB
incentive monetary policy measures, consumer prices stagnated
overall, while annual inﬂation in December constituted 0.2 percent
(Table 1.2).

The economy of Romania continued to be at the forefront within
the European Union, despite that the decrease in external demand
caused a slight slowdown in economic growth. Thus, the economy
of Romania increased by 3.7 percent compared to 3.0 percent in
2014. The industrial output increased on average by 2.7 percent,
while the construction production by 10.4 percent. At the same
time, external trade recorded a pronounced slowdown, exports
increased on average by 4.1 percent, and imports – by 7.6 percent.
The disinﬂationary pressures were relatively strong, consumer
prices decreased on average by 0.6 percent, in December 2015
decreasing by 0.9 percent compared to December 2014 (Table
1.2).


10
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
In 2015, the economy of the Russian Federation contracted by
3.7 percent. The evolution of main indicators was also negative,
industrial
output
decreased
on
average
by
3.4
percent,
construction activity by 7.0 percent, exports by 32.1 percent and
imports by 37.7 percent. During the reporting period, the average
unemployment rate was 5.6 percent and in December 2015 it was
5.8 percent. One of the most noticeable effects of the Russian
ruble depreciation was the inﬂation of two digits, reaching in
March the maximum level since 2002 of 16.9 percent.
At the
same time, the Russian Federation average annual inﬂation was
15.5 percent in 2015 (Table 1.2).
The economy of Ukraine ended 2015 in a disastrous situation and
the probability of a recovery is relatively low. The economy of
Ukraine decreased for the second consecutive year, the gross
domestic product decreasing by 9.9 percent. The industrial output
decreased on average by 13.4 percent, construction production by
14.9 percent, exports by 30.9 percent and imports by 31.2
percent. At the same time, the drought in the region determined
the decrease in agricultural production by 4.8 percent. Given the
signiﬁcant
depreciation
of
the
Ukrainian
hryvnia
and
the
annulment of subsidies to utilities services payment, consumer
prices increased in 2015 on average by 48.7 percent (Table 1.2).

Gross domestic product
In 2015, GDP recorded a decline of 0.5 percent, the annual
dynamics was by 5.1 percentage points lower than in 2014. In the
ﬁrst half of 2015, the economy of the Republic of Moldova
recorded an increase of 2.5 percent, being also supported by the
exports positive dynamic and gross added value of the ﬁnancial
sector. The contraction of the economic activity in annual terms
was observed in the second half of the year, recording in the third
and fourth quarters decreases of 3.7 and 3.3 percent respectively,
compared
to
the
same
periods
of
2014.
The
negative
development of the economic activity was mainly determined by
the drought in 2015, which reduced signiﬁcantly the performance

In this regard, the ﬁnal consumption of households
decreased by 2.3 percent compared to 2014.
The higher
uncertainty and the deterioration of the regional economic
climate determined the decrease in investment activity, so thus
the gross ﬁxed capital recorded a decline of 1.2 percent. The
negative impact of changes in inventories to GDP dynamics was
more pronounced (-2.4 percentage points). Similar to the previous
years, the government consumption has not a signiﬁcant impact
on the economic activity dynamic, recording a decrease of 0.4
percent.

national currency led to the signiﬁcant contraction of imports that
mitigated partially the negative contribution of the aforementioned

and services decreased by 4.3 percent compared to 2014. The
export of goods and services recorded an increase of 2.3 percent,
mitigating slightly the negative contribution generated by the
domestic demand to GDP dynamic. However, in the third and

embargoes for some categories of products and military conﬂict in

By categories of resources (Chart 2.2), the negative evolution of
GDP was mainly determined by the pronounced decrease of the
gross value added in agriculture. Thus, after a period of about two
years of supporting the economic activity, the agriculture recorded
in the third and fourth quarters of 2015 a decline of 17.4 and 18.3
percent, respectively, compared to the same periods of 2014, due
to the unfavourable meteorological conditions. Overall in 2015, the
gross added value in agriculture was by 13.4 percent lower than
in 2014. A negative evolution, which generated a less signiﬁcant
impact in 2015, was observed in trade, decreasing by 0.5 percent.
"Net taxes on products" component decreased by 0.8 percent
compared to 2014. Negative contribution of the aforementioned
components was mitigated by the positive dynamic of the ﬁnancial

Household consumption
In 2015, ﬁnal household consumption decreased by 2.3 percent,
thus, recording a level of 5.5 percentage points lower than in 2014.
The negative dynamic of the household consumption was mainly
determined by the decrease in expenses for the procurement of
goods. These decreased by 3.7 percent, contributing to annual
growth rate formation of ﬁnal household consumption by -2.2
percentage points (Chart 2.3).
In 2015, consumption in kind
decreased by 3.5 percent, generating a negative contribution
of 0.5 percentage points to annual growth rate formation of the
ﬁnal household consumption.

Investments
In 2015, investments in long-term tangible assets recorded a
signiﬁcant decrease, by 8.8 percent less than in 2014.
By types of ﬁxed assets, all subcomponents contributed negatively
to annual investment dynamic, excluding investments in tools,
machines and means of transport, which recorded an increase
of 1.1 percent annually (Chart 2.5). Thus, investments in other
ﬁxed assets, non-residential and residential buildings recorded
decreases of 26.7, 14.1 and 6.0 percent respectively, compared to
2014.
In terms of funding sources to achieve the investment process, the
development of the subcomponents, excluding the household’s

Agricultural production
The volume of global agricultural production decreased by 13.8
percent compared to 2014 (Chart 2.7), as a result of the decrease
in volume of the plant production by 22.6 percent, generating a
negative contribution of 14.6 percentage points to annual rate
formation.
The pronounced decrease in volume of the plant
production was mainly caused by the drought of summer 2015. At
the same time, the volume of animal production recorded an

Transport of goods
In 2015, enterprises of rail, road, river and air transport have
transported goods by 18.3 percent less than in 2014.
This
development was due to the decrease in annual terms of the
volume of goods transported by all means of transport.
Thus,
transport enterprises recorded the following annual developments:
river -33.1 percent, air -29.0 percent, auto -18.8 percent and rail
-16.7 percent (Chart 2.9).

The turnover of the enterprises having retail trade as main activity
recorded a decrease of 6.4 percent compared to 2014. At the

The annual rate of the turnover amount related to retail trade

by 3.3 percentage points lower to that of the ﬁrst half of 2015.

dynamic, but higher to that of trade in goods.

The development of exports by category of countries (Chart 2.11)
shows that in the ﬁrst half of 2015, the decrease in annual terms of
the annual rate of exports was mainly determined by the decrease
in exports to CIS countries by 37.3 percent, generating a negative
contribution of 11.9 percentage points to annual rate formation.
At the same time, in the second half of 2015, there was recorded
a negative contribution from both, CIS and EU countries.
The development of exports by group of goods (Chart 2.12) shows
that all groups of goods generated negative contributions to
annual growth rate formation of exports. Thus, in the ﬁrst half of
2015, the decrease in exports was mainly determined by the
decrease in exports of "chemical and wood products", "textile and
clothing" and "foodstuff,
products of the animal kingdom,
beverages and fats". At the same time, in the third and fourth
quarters of 2015, the group "foodstuff, products of the animal
kingdom, beverages and fats" recorded a signiﬁcant negative
contribution. Thus, the sharp decrease in exports of food products
was mainly determined by the drought of 2015, which inﬂuenced
signiﬁcantly the development of agricultural crops during the
reporting period.
During 2015, the annual growth rate of imports recorded a
downward trend, decreasing from the level -4.7 percent in the
fourth quarter of 2014 to -29.4 percent in the fourth quarter of
2015.
This development was inﬂuenced by the dynamic of
imports from all categories of countries (Chart 2.13).
Thus,
compared to 2014, imports from CIS, EU and the Rest of the World
decreased by 29.7, 23.9 and 22.0 percent, respectively.
By groups of goods (Chart 2.14), the decrease in volume of
imported goods was determined mainly by the decrease in
imports of "mineral products" (-36.4 percent), "vehicles, optical
and sound recorder and reproducers" (-26.4 percent) and
"chemical and wood products" (-22.3 percent). At the same time,
the signiﬁcant depreciation of the national currency against U.S.
dollar and the decrease in volume of transfers from abroad
inﬂuenced directly the demand for imported goods. Consequently,
the annual growth rate of imports recorded a signiﬁcant decrease
during 2015.

Labour market
Workforce
In 2015, labour market conditions have demonstrated mixed
developments. The beginning of 2015 was characterized by the
negative annual dynamic of the number of employed persons,
decreasing by 1.5 percent than in the ﬁrst quarter of 2014, but,
subsequently, the trend reversed. Thus, the annual growth rate
of employed population increased from 0.3 percent in the second
quarter of 2015 to 5.2 percent in the fourth quarter of 2015. At
the same time, the number of economically active population
increased in 2015 compared to 2014, the annual growth rate
oscillated from 2.1 percent at the beginning of the year to 6.0
percent at the end of the year (Chart 2.15). On the other hand, the
number of unemployed persons decreased from 97.9 thousand in
the ﬁrst quarter of 2015 to 51.2 thousand in the fourth quarter of
2015. However, the unemployed rate recorded during 2015 higher
values compared to 2014. At the same time, the unemployment
rate decreased from 8.5 percent in the ﬁrst quarter of 2015 to 4.2
percent in the fourth quarter of 2015, increasing by 0.7 percentage
points compared to the same period of 2014 (Chart 2.16).
The seasonally adjusted ﬁgures of unemployment rate showed
during 2015 a similar trend as the seasonally non-adjusted data.
Thus, the seasonally adjusted unemployed rate decreased from
6.8 percent in the ﬁrst quarter of 2015 to 4.2 percent in the fourth
quarter of 2015. At the same time, the employed rate increased
from 39.6 percent in the ﬁrst quarter of 2015 to 40.7 percent in
the fourth quarter of 2015.
According to data provided by the ANOFM4, the number of people
laid off fell in 2015 by 16.3 percent compared to the average of
2014, and the number of vacancies has declined by 7.0 percent.
Within the distribution of the national economy, the largest
concentration of employed population is registered in agricultural
sector, its share amounting to 31.7 percent (Chart 2.17). The
decrease in number of employed population was recorded in the
sectors: "trade" by 6.5 percent, "constructions" by 1.5 percent
and "other activities" by 3.5 percent.
The "transports and
communications" and "agricultural" sectors recorded the most
signiﬁcant increases of 12.7 and 5.8 percent,
respectively,
compared to 2014.
4The National Agency for Employment.

Wages
Wage bill in the economy increased by 9.1 percent compared
to 2014, while deﬂated by CPI decreased by 0.6 percent.
Its
annual dynamic recorded a downward trend throughout the year,
decreasing more in the second half of 2015 (Chart 2.18).
During 2015, the dynamic of the average wage in the national
economy recorded a trend similar to the wage bill in the economy.
Thus, in the ﬁrst two quarters, the annual average wage rate in
national economy was about 12.8 percent. In the second half
of 2015, it decreased signiﬁcantly so that in the forth quarter
the average wage in the national economy increased only by 6.4
percent compared to the same quarter of 2014. The budgetary and
real sector of the economy registered an increase of the average
wage by 3.7 and 7.5 percent, respectively, compared to the fourth
quarter of 2014.
The annual growth rate of the real average wage in economy
decreased during the year, amounting to -6.2 percent in the fourth
quarter of 2015 (Chart 2.19). This dynamic was driven by the
negative development of the real average wage in both budgetary
sector (-8.6 percent) and real sector (-5.2 percent).
The annual growth rate of real average wage in industry recorded
similarly a downward trajectory in 2015, amounting in the fourth
quarter to -3.0 percent.
The annual growth rate of labour
productivity in industry decreased to the amount of -3.8 percent
at the end of the year (Chart 2.20). As a result, the annual growth
rate of the labour unit cost in industry increased to the level of 1.1
percent in the fourth quarter of 2015.


18
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
2.2
Inﬂation
Consumer price index
In 2015, the annual average inﬂation rate was 9.7 percent, by 4.6
percentage points more compared to 2014. The upward inﬂation
evolution was determined mainly by the depreciation trend of
the national currency against U.S. dollar, which had a signiﬁcant
impact on imported goods and on inﬂationary expectations of
the population and economic agents.
At the same time, the
drought of 2015 exerted inﬂationary pressures on food prices.
A signiﬁcant impact on inﬂation acceleration, particularly in
the

second half of the year, was generated by the increase in electricity
tariffs in July 2015. The factors, which have compensated to some
extent the inﬂationary inﬂuence, are represented by the modest
domestic demand, embargoes on certain categories of products
and downward trend of food and oil prices on international market.
During 2015, the annual inﬂation rate had a pronounced upward
trajectory. This increased from 4.7 percent in January to 7.1 percent
in March, exceeding thus the upper limit of the range of variation of
±1.5 percentage points from 5.0 percent inﬂation target stipulated
in the Medium-term monetary policy strategy. Subsequently, the
annual inﬂation rate increased gradually to 8.6 percent in July
2015. The increase in electricity tariffs determined a pronounced
inﬂation acceleration up to 12.2 percent in August, while at the
end of 2015 the inﬂation rate was 13.6 percent.
In 2015, core inﬂation continued its upward trend started in 2013,
recording more pronounced rates. Its annual rate increased to
14.6 percent in December 2015, by 7.6 percentage point higher
than in January, under the pressure exerted by the depreciation
of the national currency against U.S. dollar. The depreciation and
the modest harvest of 2015 determined the increase in annual
rate of food prices from 4.5 percent in January to 14.3 percent
at the end of 2015 (Chart 2.22). Prices on regulated goods and
services recorded a slight upward dynamic in the ﬁrst half of
the year.
Following the adjustment of electricity tariffs, these
increased signiﬁcantly to 13.4 percent in August, oscillating around
the respective level by the end of 2015. The annual dynamic of fuel
prices recorded a relatively stable development in the ﬁrst seven
months of the year, accounting to about 5.5 percent. Therefore,
the annual rate of fuel prices decelerated to about 2.6 percent in
the last ﬁve months of 2015, under the inﬂuence of the decrease
in prices of energy resources.
Prices for core inﬂation (on average by 4.1 percentage points)
contributed the most to the annual average inﬂation rate
formation (Chart 2.23).
Food prices contributed by about 3.5
percentage points, while prices for regulated services determined
a contribution of 1.8 percentage points. Fuel prices generated the
lowest contribution (0.3 percentage points) to annual average
inﬂation dynamic in 2015.
Core inﬂation
During 2015, the annual rate of core inﬂation recorded an upward
trajectory, increasing from 7.0 percent in January up to 14.6
percent in December.

by the increase in prices of the subcomponents "clothing", "means

and cosmetics articles" etc. (Chart 2.24).
The price increases were mainly generated by the depreciation of
national currency against the currencies of major trading partners

that internal demand has not generated inﬂationary pressures on
prices for core inﬂation in 2015.
Food prices
The evolution of the annual growth rate of food prices during 2015
was mainly inﬂuenced by the persistence of some inﬂationary
factors. Thus, the pronounced depreciation of the national currency
against USD and the dry meteorological conditions in summer 2015
contributed signiﬁcantly to the increase in annual growth rate of
food prices. Thus, this recorded in December 2015 a level of 14.3
percent or by 9.8 percentage points higher than in January 2015
(Chart 2.25).

Global food prices recorded for the third consecutive year negative
growth rates, generating thus disinﬂationary pressures on food
prices on domestic market. Thus, it decreased by 18.7 percent
compared to 2014. It should be mentioned that the disinﬂationary
pressures generated by the global food prices have been offset
by the domestic inﬂationary factors (drought, depreciation of the
national currency against U.S. dollar).
To the acceleration of the food prices annual growth rate during
2015 contributed more the increase in prices of the groups "fresh
vegetables", "potatoes", "milling and bakery products", "ﬁsh and
ﬁsh products", "fats", "sugar" and "fresh fruits" (Chart 2.25).
Regulated prices
The annual rate of regulated prices recorded in the ﬁrst seven
months of 2015 a slight upward dynamic, increasing from 1.9
percent in January to 4.3 percent in July.
The respective
development was determined by the increase in medicine prices
as a result of the national currency depreciation. Subsequently,

Fuel prices
In the ﬁrst half of 2015, the annual growth rate of fuel prices
recorded a relatively stable development but subsequently
decreased signiﬁcantly in the second half of the year. In the ﬁrst
semester of 2015, the annual average rate of fuel prices oscillated
around the value of 5.3 percent (Chart 2.27). During the ﬁrst six
months of 2015, the rise of prices for "earth coal" subcomponent
contributed the most to the annual rate formation of fuel prices.
This development was inﬂuenced by the military conﬂict in

Eastern Ukraine that affected the coal mining and its offer. In the
second half of 2015, the evolution of fuel prices recorded a
downward dynamic, recording in July-December an average
annual rate of 3.7 percent, by 1.6 percentage points lower than in
January-June. The slowdown in annual growth rate of fuel prices
was mainly determined by the disappearance of pressures from
earth coal prices.
At the same time, the contribution from
ﬁrewood prices to annual growth rate formation increased and
their prices have risen as a result of the higher coal price. On the
other hand, the contribution from fuel prices decreased in the
second half of 2015, as a result of the signiﬁcant decrease in
international oil prices. Thus, the price of Urals brand oil in the
second semester of 2015 reached an average of USD 45.5 per
barrel, decreasing by 20.3 percent compared with the ﬁrst
semester of 2015.
In July-December 2015, the impact of the
decrease in oil prices was however mitigated, to some extent, by
the depreciation of national currency against U.S. dollar.

the ﬁrst three months of 2015 an upward trajectory, subsequently
recording a downward trend in the next three months. Thus, this
recorded in June 2015 a level of 3.4 percent or by 2.8 percentage
points lower than in March 2015. At the same time, in the second

Construction prices
During 2015, the annual growth rate of construction prices
oscillated around the value of 8.0 percent. In the fourth quarter of
2015, this amounted to 8.2 percent, decreasing by 0.1 percentage
points from the fourth quarter of 2014 (Chart 2.30).
Within the structure of the national economy, in the fourth quarter
of 2015, the following sectors have recorded the largest increases
compared to the same period of 2014: "building construction"
(12.7 percent), "construction of social and cultural objects" (8.0
percent),
"trade
and
catering"
(7.4
percent)
and
"telecommunications" (6.6 percent).
Producer prices for agricultural products
Producer prices for agricultural products increased by 22.5 percent
compared to 2014. This development was mainly determined by
the increase in prices of plant products (34.4 percent). At the same
time, the prices of livestock products decreased in annual terms
by 8.9 percent (Chart 2.31).
The development of plant products prices was determined mainly
by the increase in prices of "fruits and berries" (76.6 percent),
"sunﬂower" (46.7 percent), "grape" (42.2 percent), "rape" (40.2
percent) and "cereals and grain legumes" (25.3 percent). It should
be mentioned that the decrease in prices of livestock products
was determined by the decrease in prices of "pork meat" (20.0
percent) and "poultry meat" (2.9 percent).

Public sector
In 2015, the national public budget indicators recorded nominal
increases, below the annual duties set out, consequently the public
income and expenditure increased and the budget deﬁcit was
within permissible limits.
According to the Ministry of Finance, the dynamic of total national
public budget revenues recorded a slowdown in annual growth

Relative to GDP, revenue collection decreased by 2.1 percentage

Similar to previous periods, tax-related revenue held the largest

tax-related revenue was mainly inﬂuenced by the VAT revenue that
recorded annual growth rate of 6.7 percent, with a share of 36.0

social insurance and medical insurance premiums) that increased
on average by 12.4 percent annually, with a share of 31.8, while
tax income increased by 13.9 percent compared to 2014 and

(Chart 2.33).

mainly by the increase in expenditure allocated to social insurance
and assistance by MDL 1 537.1 million compared to 2014 or by
10.9 percent, education - MDL 638.5 million or by 8.2 percent,
health protection - by MDL 565.2 million or by 9.6 percent.

At the end of 2015, the state debt of the Republic of Moldova
amounted to MDL 33 509.2 million, increasing by MDL 6 038.6
million or by 22.0 percent more than at the end of 2014. This
debt consists mainly of external state debt, with a share of 78.4
percent of the total debt and internal state debt with a share
of 21.6 percent of the total debt. The balance of external state
debt expressed in foreign currency increased by USD 30.9 million,
amounting at the end of the reporting period to USD 1 337.0
million. External state debt, expressed in MDL, increased during
2015 by 28.9 percent, amounting to MDL 26 283.8 million at the
end of December. Internal state debt recorded the value of MDL
7 225.4 million, by MDL 150.0 million higher than at the end of
2014, or by 2.1 percent. At the end of 2015, this component was
composed of SS issued on the primary market in the amount of
MDL 5 162.0 million and SS converted - MDL 2 063.4 million. It
should be mentioned that in 2015 the SS for ﬁnancial stability in
the amount of MDL 93.1 million were entirely redeemed.
In 2015, the ﬁnancing means from the national public budget in
the amount MDL 1043.3 million (by 76.3 percent more than in
2014) were held by the state debt service, of which 76.1 percent
were held by internal state debt and external state debt - 23.9
percent.

International

of
the
Republic
of

for
2015
(provisional data)
Balance of payments7
During 2015, the external sector of the Republic of Moldova
economy was marked by an economic recession recorded by CIS
main partners (Russian Federation and Ukraine) that had a
signiﬁcant negative impact on export and personal remittances.
The application of the EU-Moldova Deep and Comprehensive Free
Trade Area (DCFTA) since 1 September 2014 had a positive impact
on the Republic of Moldova exports to EU (which increased by USD
44.18 million or by 6.1 percent compared to 2014, except the
goods for/after processing),
but was not sufﬁcient to fully
compensate the decrease in exports to CIS countries (which
decreased by USD 233.34 million or by 32.2 percent compared to
2014, except for the goods for/after processing).
Personal
remittances received by the Republic of Moldova residents
decreased by 24.1 percent as a share to GDP in 2014 to 21.8
percent in 2015, mainly due to the decrease in remittances from
the Russian Federation.
The decrease of incomes received by residents from abroad
contributed
to
the
decrease
in
domestic
demand
and,
respectively,
in
import
of
consumer
goods
and
services
(particularly travel services). The slowdown in economic activity
and low oil prices determined the decrease in import of the raw
materials and of the capital goods by the economic agents.
Thus, in 2015 (according to preliminary data), the net ﬁnancial
borrowing required (the sum of the balance of the current
account and that of the capital account in the balance of payments)
decreased by 6.0 percent compared to 2014 and amounted to
USD 441.41 million, representing 6.8 percent as a share to GDP
(5.9 percent in 2014), as a result of combined inﬂuence of the
aforementioned factors (Table A.1).

dynamics may be accessed at the following link:
http://bnm.md/en/content/
international-accounts-republic-moldova-2015-preliminary-data.

2014.

In 2015, the balance of services recorded a surplus in the
amount of USD 143.91 million, increasing by 19.5 percent
compared to 2014, as a result of the lower decrease in value of
services rendered to non-residents by 13.5 percent compared to
the decrease rate in services received from non-residents - by
17.5 percent. The ratio between the balance of services surplus
and GDP was 2.2 percent, increasing by 0.7 percentage points
compared with 2014 (Table A.2).
The economic slowdown affected mainly the volume of external
trade in transport services, particularly the export and import
of freight services, which decreased by 18.9 percent and 24.9
percent, respectively, as a result of the decrease in volume of
external trade in goods. The imports of travel services have also
decreased signiﬁcantly (-20.3 percent), mainly due to the decrease
in economic migration of population. The exports of raw material
processing services were also affected by the crisis, decreasing by
14.3 percent compared to 2014.
Primary income surplus in 2015 decreased twice, to USD 402.68
million, being determined by the compensation of resident
employees for the work performed for non-resident employers,
which decreased by 27.0 percent. At the same time, the incomes
payable of foreign investors has increased (+49.0 percent). The
reserve assets incomes amounted to USD 23.8 million, decreasing
by 20.8 percent compared to 2014 (Table A.3). The ratio between
the balance of primary incomes and GDP was 6.2 percent,
decreasing by 4.2 percentage points compared with 2014.


Chapter 2. The economic situation of the Republic of Moldova in 2015
27
Secondary income recorded a surplus in the amount of USD
1077.30 million, which decreased by 30.1 percent compared to
2014, amid the decrease by 27.8 percent of secondary income
inﬂows (mainly due to the decrease by 25.8 percent of personal
transfers received by residents from abroad), in a higher proportion
of 3.8 percent than the outﬂows rate. The decrease in secondary
income inﬂows was also inﬂuenced by the decrease in volume of
grants and in external technical assistance offered to the Republic
of Moldova (current international cooperation), both at the level
of public administration (by 2.7 times) and private level (-18.2
percent). The ratio between the secondary income balance and
GDP was 16.6 percent (Table A.4).
The value of personal remittances (a new indicator calculated
additionally to the balance of payments in accordance with the
BPM6 methodology), which reﬂects the value of net compensation
for work (excluding the expenses for travelling abroad and certain
fees paid by residents abroad), of personal transfers and that of
capital between the households, amounted to USD 1 408.57 million
(21.8 percent to GDP), decreasing by 26.7 percent compared to
2014.
Capital account recorded in 2015 a positive amount of USD
22.83 million. The public administration received from abroad
grants related to investment projects in the amount of USD 89.34
million, while other sectors recorded net outﬂows in the amount
of USD 66.43 million. In 2015, the ﬁnancial account8 recorded
net capital inﬂows in the amount of USD 473.18 million, due to the
decrease in ﬁnancial assets by USD 259.31 million and increase
in liabilities by USD 213.87 million (Chart 2.42).

during 2015.

2015, external debt is divided by institutional sectors as following:
other sectors - 50.5 percent, governmental sector - 21.4 percent,
liabilities against foreign direct investors - 17.2 percent, banking
sector - 5.5 percent, monetary authorities - 5.4 percent.

has been held by: IBRD and IDA - 49.8 percent, IMF - 25.3 percent,
EIB - 12.7 percent, IFAD - 5.4 percent. The liabilities against the
bilateral creditors totalled USD 192.22 million, increasing by 12.3
percent compared to 31 December 2014. The share of Japan -28.6
percent, Russian Federation - 27.6 percent, USA - 19.8 percent is
also included in the debt structure against bilateral creditors.


31
Chapter 3
The activitity of the National Bank of
Moldova in 2015
3.1
Achievements of the monetary and
foreign exchange policy
In accordance with the Law on the National Bank of Moldova
no.548-XIII of 21 July 1995, the NBM is the central bank of the
Republic of Moldova, being the autonomous public legal entity
that
establishes
and
promotes
the
monetary
and
foreign
exchange policy. During 2015, the NBM acted in accordance with
the Medium-term monetary policy strategy, approved by the
Council of Administration of the National Bank of Moldova on 27
December 2012.
According to this strategy, in order to ensure and maintain price
stability, the NBM has set its inﬂation target, measured by the
consumer price index published monthly by the National Bureau
of Statistics (NBS) of 5.0 percent with a possible deviation of ± 1.5
percentage points.
Quantifying the macroeconomic situation, trends and projections
of macroeconomic indicators in the medium-term, inﬂation outlook
in the short and medium-term, with possible uncertainties and
challenges during the reference period, as well as to anchor
inﬂationary pressures, the National Bank of Moldova has promoted
during 2015 a restrictive monetary policy. At the same time, the
monetary policy stance was affected by the complexity of risk
balance, both internal and external, with a gradual accentuation
of inﬂationary risks. The external risks are propagated through
the channel of remittances in favour of population and external
trade, leading to lower foreign currency income of population and
domestic exporters in the short-term, which was reﬂected in the
further inﬂation dynamic.
In these circumstances, the National Bank of Moldova had to
adopt gradual monetary policy tightening measures to reduce
inﬂationary pressures from regulated prices and national currency
depreciation. In this context, the members of the Executive Board
of the NBM decided to increase during 2015 the base rate by 13.0
percentage points, from 6.5 to 19.5 percent annually.
These decisions were aimed at anchoring inﬂation expectations in
the context of restoring and maintaining the inﬂation rate close
to the target of 5.0 percent, with a possible deviation of ± 1.5
percentage points.


32
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
Implementation and promotion of monetary and foreign
exchange policy during 2015
The NBM reaction to the increasing risk of the inﬂationary
expectations deterioration was a ﬁrm one, consisting of the
progressive accentuation of growth trend of the base rate
restrictiveness initiated in December 2014, aimed to decrease the
inﬂationary pressures of the demand and to reanchor the
inﬂationary
expectations
to
a
level
compatible
with
the
medium-term inﬂation target achievement.
Thus, the NBM operated six increasing adjustments of monetary
policy rate, its level increasing from 6.50 to 19.50 percent: the
trend initiated by increasing the rate by 2.0 and 5.0 percentage
points in January and February, which was temporarily interrupted
and continued in May by operating four consecutive increasing
adjustments that cumulated 6.0 percentage points. In September
2015, NBM decided to interrupt the upward trend of the monetary
policy rate and maintain its level to 19.50 percent until the end of
2015.
The increase in interest rate on state securities (SS) with the
maturity of 91 days was determined by the maintenance of the
ﬁrm orientation of the liquidity control policy and the increase
of the monetary policy rate. However, the yields irregularities
from the level of monetary policy rate were more visible compared
to 2014, being almost of the year above the upper limit of the
policy rate corridor of the NBM. Recording at the end of 2014
an upward trajectory, the interest rate on SS with the maturity
of 91 days recorded a more pronounced increase in March 2015
(recording a double value compared to February), exceeding the
upper limit of the rates corridor. Subsequently, this rate recorded
a slight correction, returning temporarily within the rate corridor
limits. However, it recorded in July an upward trend, being followed
by a relative stabilisation by the end of the year and reached a
maximum level of 23.89 percent in December.
The interest rates of interbank loans/deposits were more rigid in
terms of their role in monetary transmission mechanism, reﬂecting
the speciﬁc situation created in the banking system, recording
in certain periods even the lack of activity in this segment. The
monthly average interest rate of interbank credits/deposits varied
during 2015 within the range of 9.51 - 20.50 percent annually.
Monetary policy instruments
Monetary conditions adequacy in the light of the monetary policy
objective was achieved through the interest rate policy, being
supported by the management of liquidity by the central bank.
In the latter, market operations, standing facilities and required
reserve mechanism continued to play the main role.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
33
Open market operations
The banks’ net position of liquidity deﬁcit at the end of 2014
continued to be maintained at the beginning of 2015, being
signiﬁcantly accentuated in February-March. During the following
periods, the main role in liquidity management and interest rates
inﬂuence has been taken up by the monetary absorbing operations.

Issuance of NBM Certiﬁcates
The National Bank of Moldova conducted twice a week NBM
Certiﬁcates placement auctions, with the maturity of 14 days,
announcing the maximum interest rate equivalent to the NBM
base rate. Thus, the NBM absorbed fully the amounts offered by
participants.
The average weighted rate of sterilization operations was 15.45
percent annually, compared to 3.51 percent registered in 2014.
Amid the registration of a liquidity excess almost of 2015, the
volume of liquidity-absorbing operations decreased by 50.0
percent compared to 2014, a situation also reﬂected in annual
average of NBM Certiﬁcates stock, which decreased by MDL 840.5
million and recorded MDL 783.4 million.
The balance of NBM
Certiﬁcates amounted to MDL 612.3 million at the end of 2015.
Repo operations
Repo operations were conducted via auctions at a ﬁxed rate (base
rate plus a margin of 0.25 percentage points) and full allocation.
These operations were weekly conducted until March with the term
of 28 days. Following the rise of liquidity deﬁcit, at the beginning
of March, the NBM increased the ongoing frequency (twice a week)
and decreased the maturity of repo operations (to 14 days).
Banks’ liquidity demand was fully satisﬁed, decreasing signiﬁcantly
during the reporting year. Thus, the balance of repo operations
recorded at the end of the reporting year (MDL 180.0 million)
was signiﬁcantly lower to 2014 (MDL 1 097.0 million). Given that
the liquidity volume through repo operations was double in 2015
compared to that of 2014, their annual average amounted to MDL
538.8 million compared to MDL 292.4 million in 2014.
Lending activity
According
to
the
terms
of
the
emergency
loan
contracts
concluded in 2014 and 2015 with Banca de Economii S.A., BC
„BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A., the NBM granted
during January-October 2015 emergency loans in the total amount
of MDL 7 771.7 million.
Pursuant to Article 385(1) letter a) of the Law on Financial
Institutions no.
550-XIII of 24 July 2015, on the date of the
withdrawal of licenses for ﬁnancial activity of Banca de Economii
S.A., BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A., on 16
October 2015, the calculation of interests on liabilities of the
aforementioned banks was interrupted, while the liabilities related
to emergency loans granted to these banks, unmatured, have
reached their maturity.


34
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
In these circumstances, as of 16 October 2015, the balance of
outstanding loans of Banca de Economii S.A., BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A”
S.A. and B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A. amounted to MDL 14 145.1 million,
while the balance of calculated and outstanding interests - MDL
8.8 million.
In the last two months of 2015, based on the decision of the
NBM Executive Board, the banks in the process of liquidation paid
entirely the interests related to emergency loans and partially the
outstanding emergency loans in the amount of MDL 432.5 million.
During 2015, at the end of each quarter, Banca de Economii S.A.
paid the last four tranches in the total amount of MDL 93.1 million
of the loan granted by the NBM for the protection of the banking
system integrity. The loan was paid by Banca de Economii S.A.
from the means obtained from redemption of government bonds
issued by the Ministry of Finance and transferred to bank in order
to take over the receivables due by BC „Investprivatbank” S.A.
under the liquidation process.
Loans granted by the NBM in 1992-2002 for crediting the
construction of housing cooperatives (CHC), with the ﬁnal
reimbursement term in 2019, continued to be monitored by the
NBM during 2015. As a result of the payment of matured tranches
and the anticipated loan payments, at the end of the reporting
year the balance of credits granted to banks for CHC lending
decreased by about 87.0 percent compared to the end of 2014,
amounting to MDL 0.7 million as of 31 December 2015 (Table A.9).
Thus, as of 31 December 2015, the credit indebtedness of the
banks to National Bank of Moldova constituted MDL 13 689.9
million, being represented by emergency outstanding loans (MDL
13 689.2 million) and loans granted for crediting the construction
of housing cooperatives (MDL 0.7 million).
As a result of the loans portfolio assessment made in December
2015, no impairment allowances were accounted for the loans
granted, given that the emergency outstanding loans of the NBM
portfolio are under state guarantee.
Standing facilities
In 2015, the standing facilities regime (overnight deposits and
credits) set by the NBM, allowed banks to manage their liquidity
efﬁciently and with more ﬂexibility.
Over the whole analyzed year, simultaneously with the base rate,
the standing facilities rates have been changed 6 times, the
increases level being between 1.0 and 5.0 percentage points.
Thus, in 2015, the interest rate on overnight deposits evolved
from 5.50 percent annually at the beginning of the year to 16.50
percent annually at the end of the year, and the overnight credit -
from 11.50 percent annually to 22.50 percent annually.
The
interest
rates
on
standing
facilities
continued
to
form
a
symmetrical corridor of ±3.0 percentage points compared to the
base rate.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
35
Overnight deposit facility
In 2015, all banks placed overnight deposits with the National Bank
of Moldova.
The daily average balance of banks’ overnight placements
calculated for each month recorded nonuniform values, between
the maximum of MDL 988.3 million in January and the minimum of
MDL 346.5 million in October, the monthly volume of overnight
deposits reﬂecting mainly the development of the excessive
liquidity level in the system.
The volume of overnight deposits placed by banks at the National
Bank in the reporting year amounted to MDL 136 503.7 million, by
about 2.5 times more than in 2014.
The signiﬁcant increase in volume of overnight placements
compared to 2014 occurred due to the increase of liquidities in
the banking system and to more effective management of excess
liquidity by banks, ensured by the development of interest rates
to this instrument.
During the maintenance periods of required reserves in MDL,
banks resorted to deposit facility with a similar frequency:
constituting minimum amounts at the beginning of the period,
overnight placements have increased as the excess liquidity
gained by banks ensured the obligation to comply with the set
level of required reserves, reaching the maximum levels at the
end of period.
Overnight credit facility
In 2015, banks resorted sporadically to overnight credit facility
compared to 2014, while in May-August and November the facility
was unsolicited.
The amount of overnight loans granted by the NBM during 2015
was MDL 3 481.8 million, which indicates a daily average balance
of MDL 13.1 million, lower by 5 times than that of 2014.
In 2015, the overnight credit facility was solicited mainly at the end
of maintenance periods of the required reserves by a restrained
number of banks, which failed to comply with the requirements
set for the required reserves regime.
Required reserves
In 2015, the required reserves mechanism had an important role
to ensure the austerity of promoted monetary policy, which was
manifested in rigorous monetary control and prudent liquidity
management in banking system.
Over the whole year, the required reserves ratio in MDL increased
gradually from 14.0 to 35.0 percent of the base, the increasing
decisions being adopted in most of the cases simultaneously with
those to increase the NBM monetary policy rates.
In the reporting year, the upward development of required reserves
ratio in MDL is as follows:


36
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
• for the maintenance period of required reserves 8 February
2015 - 7 March 2015, the required reserves ratio was
increased by 2.0 percentage points and established at the
level of 16.0 percent of the base;
• starting with the maintenance period of required reserves 8
March 2015 - 7 April 2015, the required reserves ratio was
increased by 2.0 percentage points and established at the
level of 18.0 percent of the base;

Intervention on the domestic foreign exchange market
In 2015, NBM intervened on the domestic foreign exchange market,
mainly as a seller of foreign currency, in the light of the monetary
policy promoted by the NBM and to mitigate excessive ﬂuctuations
of the exchange rate of the national currency against the U.S.
dollar.
