Monetary policy communication and transparency
During 2015, the NBM communication activity in the ﬁeld of
monetary policy enhanced its position, able to meet the central
bank’s objective to ensure and maintain the price stability in the
light of direct inﬂation targeting regime, being aimed at keeping
the public properly informed.
The increasing of monetary policy transparency along with the
diversiﬁcation and improvement of communication channels by
different type of target-group constituted the NBM challenge and
effort during 2015.
In order to ensure the transparency of monetary policy decisions,
the NBM has published press releases on the day of the ordinary
meetings, including the extraordinary meeting (17 February 2015)
of the Council of Administration and Executive Board of the NBM on
monetary policy, which were related to the base rate and required
reserves, as well as to the factors and premises that were the basis
of the respective decisions.
At the same time, the NBM has published monthly press releases
on inﬂation, containing information about inﬂation rate and its
components and the challenges that might affect the inﬂationary
process in the context of maintaining price stability.
In order to explain to the general public, the analytical and
implicit framework of monetary policy conduct, the NBM has
quarterly published during 2015 the Inﬂation Report, which
contains
the
analysis
of
the
most
recent
macroeconomic
developments, inﬂation medium-term forecasts (2 years), the risk
and uncertainty analyses for achieving the quantitative target and
the Summaries of meetings of the Council of Administration and
Executive Board on monetary policy.
In order to ensure a greater understanding of the value of projected
inﬂation, the NBM has organized on the day of publication of
10With licensed banks from the Republic of Moldova.


38
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
the inﬂation reports, press conferences with the participation
of the Governor of the NBM and the media, where the external
economic situation and the domestic economic development had
been explained, which substantiated the medium-term forecast.
Similarly, to the practice of other central banks, National Bank
continued to support the ﬁnancial education, organizing on 28
September 2015 an interactive session hosted by the Economic
and Financial College in Chis, in˘au with the participation of pupils
from third and fourth year and college teachers. The conference
on „Monetary policy of the National Bank of Moldova" aimed at
increasing the NBM transparency relating to the implementation
and promotion of monetary policy, thus contributing to a better
knowledge and understanding of monetary policy decisions and
interdependencies between the various macroeconomic processes
in the Republic of Moldova.
The NBM ofﬁcial website was improved and a new version was
launched in June 2015. The improvement of the structure and
format for presenting the information on the NBM ofﬁcial website
through new functionalities and technical facilities contributed
to the optimization of the external communication with different
target-groups. In the light of information content, it was updated
and extended by the publication of technical boxes, statistical data,
infographics. The interactive basis of the ofﬁcial website received
much public attention.
The NBM representatives have participated in various TV and radio
broadcasts, as well as published articles in newspapers / Internet
on topics related to monetary policy.
In 2015, the National Bank of Moldova has further responded to all
requests and petitions received from the general public. Main
topics of public interest were related to the base rate dynamics
during certain periods and the similarity of base rate and
reﬁnancing rate phrases.
On the basis that the great challenge of monetary policy consists
in ensuring a complex, diversiﬁed and extensive communication
- commitments stipulated in the Medium-term monetary policy
strategy, item XI Monetary policy communication, the NBM will
pursue the development of an effective communication system
in the context of improving communication tools and channels,
capable of ensuring a predictable, full and clear communication in
order to anchor inﬂation expectations to the inﬂation target.
Research, analysis and forecast
In 2015, there were carried out more studies and researches within
the NBM, which aimed at developing and enhancing the framework
for the analysis and forecast of the inﬂation and economic activity
necessary to substantiate the monetary policy decisions and to
implement effectively the inﬂation targeting strategy. Main topics
addressed within studies were concentrated on some properties
of the CPI inﬂation subcomponents, the impact of investments on
economic growth, dollarization phenomena and its implications,
relationship between work remuneration and inﬂation etc. Some
of these researches provided signiﬁcant results and conclusions


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
39
that will facilitate the analysis and forecast process, while others
required additional efforts and will be continued in next periods.
For example, the price seasonality phenomena and its impact
on inﬂation volatility in the Republic of Moldova was analysed
during the year.
There was established in the framework of
the study that the consumer price index covers subcomponents
and irregularities such as the seasonality that makes difﬁcult the
analysis of inﬂationary pressures and the foundation of monetary
policy decisions.
The annual inﬂation indicator eliminates to
some extent these deﬁciencies. However, the monthly inﬂation is
also used in the decision-making process and for communication
purposes, which must be seasonally adjusted ﬁrst in order to
offer relevant information to the monetary policy. Therefore, the
seasonal factor has moderate positive values in the ﬁrst four
months of the year, after which it becomes signiﬁcantly negative
in the summer months. In autumn and December, the seasonal
factor turns positive. At the same time, the highest impact on
seasonal ﬂuctuations is determined by the seasonal factor of food
prices.
Certain summaries of the studies carried out during 2015 on
factors and phenomena with an impact on the inﬂation or economic
activity development were published in the Inﬂation Report in the
form of technical boxes. In this regard, there were analysed the
consequences of the changes in exercise duties and tariffs for
regulated prices on inﬂation. It was also investigated the impact
of the Russian ruble depreciation on prospects for the dynamic
of remittances and economic activity of the Republic of Moldova.
Another important aspect, which was addressed, represents the
role of foreign investments for the development of a state and
the economic situation of the Republic of Moldova relating to this
aspect in comparison with other countries in the region.
In 2015, the identiﬁcation and analysis of relevant factors for the
inﬂation process in the Republic of Moldova continued. In this
regard,
the
degree
of
inﬂuence
of
internal
and
external
macroeconomic variables on the growth of certain subcomponents
of the CPI have been re-estimated by means of statistical and
econometric methods. Subsequently, this information was used in
the four rounds of forecasting for achieving short-term inﬂation
projections, key constituent of the framework for the analysis and
forecast of the inﬂation targeting regime. Additionally, in order to
improve the accuracy of CPI short-term forecast, during 2015,
were further monitored the prices of a basket of food products and
non-food products in the CPI in some commercial units in Chis, in˘au,
obtaining important operational data in time on price dynamics
and its possible causes. At the same time, the Inﬂation Reports
included assessments of short-term forecast deviation from actual
data, pointing out the main underlying objective factors.
In order to identify and quantify the pressures from aggregate
demand on inﬂation during 2015, the activity for estimating the
economic activity and its potential level was also pursued.
In
this respect, the econometric equations for the subcomponents of
GDP uses were re-estimated, based on a number of external and
internal assumptions, such as the economic activity in the EU and
the Russian Federation, the household disposable income or the
performance of agricultural sector.
During 2015, there was attempted to develop a model of the retail
interest rates on deposits and credits. By this developing model,


40
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
there were assessed the conduct of the retail interest rates and
identiﬁed
the
interest
rate
channel
particularities
of
the
transmission mechanism (interest rate pass-trough), which is a
signiﬁcant
segment
of
the
monetary
policy
transmission
mechanism.
The developed model allows to assess the real
monetary conditions through the retail interest rates.
In order to have in real-time a complete picture on forecast of
economic activity of the Republic of Moldova, there was developed
a model with common dynamic factors, which is based on the
model suggested by Stock and Watson (1991).
An operative
indicator is generated based on this model, which increases the
perception degree of the economic activity and anticipates its
trends. The analysis based on the model with common dynamic
factors is easy to interpret and may be easy updated.
Given the environment increasing volatility, in 2015, there were
assessed the factors of the low international oil prices in 2014-
2015, being analysed the impact of this phenomena on domestic
market under the conditions of the national currency exchange
rate evolution.
In 2015, the regional economy recorded an anemic development
and the challenges related to national currency and external trade
development being signiﬁcant. Although, various particularities
existed from a country to another, the engine of the crisis in the
region constituted the Russian-Ukrainian geopolitical conﬂict,
which had enormous repercussions on economies of the countries
concerned and in the whole region. The dispersed impact was
analysed through the external trade channel, ﬁnancial channel,
remittance channel and energy channel in order to better
understand this phenomenon.
Given that a large part of food prices in the Republic of Moldova are
imported, the prices of origin countries inﬂuence the domestic food
prices. Although, the calculations, aggregate index worldwide and
the index calculated and published by FAO (Food and Agriculture
Organization) are used easily in the analyses, these have not
a signiﬁcant correlation with food consumer price index from
the Republic of Moldova. It may be explained by the fact that
most of quotations included in this index are not relevant for the
Republic of Moldova. Therefore, there was carried out in 2015 a
detailed analysis of structure of the food imports by country of
origin, information further used for the construction of a complex
index, which has a more signiﬁcant correlation with the food
consumer price index for the Republic of Moldova. The research
included more indicators such as food consumer price index, food
production price index, FAO index, share of food import by country
and were used more methods of combination of the respective
indexes in calculations to obtain the best correlations.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
41
3.2
The
results
of
monetary
and
foreign exchange policy
The implementation of monetary policy of the National Bank of
Moldova recorded multiple achievements during the last years
and it should be mentioned that in 2009-2014 the annual inﬂation
rate was on the level of a single digit, while in February 2012
- December 2014 has fallen within the variation range of ± 1.5
percentage points from the inﬂation target of 5.0 percent.
In 2015, the monetary policy conduct was affected by the
complexity of the balance of risks with the prevalence of
inﬂationary ones. The consolidation of inﬂationary climate was
based on a more pronounced depreciation of MDL since the
beginning of the year, determined by the deep gap between the
demand and supply of foreign currency. This was caused by the
decrease in supply of foreign currency, amid the embargoes
imposed by the Russian Federation on exports of Moldovan
products and the decrease in transfers of foreign currency in
favour of individuals.
The nervousness on foreign exchange
market was also determined, to some extent, by the NBM decision
to establish special administration over Banca de Economii S.A.,
BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A. This created
conditions
for
the
negative
anticipation
and
perceptions
associated with future exchange rate dynamics of MDL and
inﬂation.
Thus, in January-February 2015, the NBM increased the base rate
applied on the main short-term monetary policy operations from
the level of 6.5 percent annually to the level of 13.5 percent
annually and the interest rate on overnight loans and deposits up
to the level of 16.5 percent annually and 10.5 percent annually,
respectively.
Taking into consideration the previous monetary
policy decisions and the propagation velocity of these decisions’
effects in the economy, the members of Council of Administration
of the NBM decided, within the meetings in March and April 2015,
to maintain the monetary policy rate at the level of 13.5 percent
annually.
In May 2015,
it was observed a gradual accentuation of
inﬂationary risks. The national currency depreciation since the
beginning of 2015 has increased the inﬂationary pressure, the
weak economic activity in the euro area countries and the
recession of the Russian Federation - the main trading partners of
the Republic of Moldova maintain the risk of lowering of foreign
currency income of households and domestic exporters in
short-term, through foreign trade and remittances channel and
the escalation of geopolitical tension in the region could also
cause additional inﬂationary pressures. Against this background,
within the meetings held in May - August 2015, the members of
the Executive Board of the NBM decided to increase the monetary
policy interest rate by 6.0 percentage points from 13.5 to 19.5
percent annually.
In September - December 2015, the National Bank of Moldova
maintained the monetary policy interest rate at the level of 19.5


42
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
percent annually, the decisions of the Executive Board of the NBM
based on the fact that the monetary policy measures adopted by
the NBM at the beginning of 2015 are to be transposed into the
national
economy
through
various
channels,
including
by
inﬂuencing interest rates on loans and deposits in national
currency, thus, exerting further effects on inﬂation development.
In order to sterilize liquidity excess accumulated during 2015 and
improve the transmission mechanism of monetary policy decisions,
in the context of ensuring an appropriate set of the monetary
conditions aimed at achieving convergence of inﬂation rate to the
target in the medium-term, the National Bank of Moldova increased
the minimum required reserves ratio from the means attracted
in MDL and non-convertible currency by 21.0 percentage points,
from the level of 14.0 percent to 35.0 percent. At the same time,
the minimum required reserve ratio from the means attracted in
freely convertible currency was maintained at the level of 14.0
percent during 2015.
This decision was aimed at anchoring inﬂation expectations in
the context of maintaining the inﬂation close to the target of 5.0
percent, with a possible deviation of ± 1.5 percentage points.
Dynamics of monetary indicators
During 2015, the monetary indicators recorded a downward trend.
It should be mentioned that in January - September 2015, the
average growth rate of money supply was 3.3 percent in annual
terms, while in the last quarter of the year was negative, recording
in October - December 2015 the level of -2.8 percent.

Money supply
In 2015, money supply (M2)11 decreased by 9.2 percent, being by
5.0 percentage points lower compared to 2014 (Chart 3.7). During
the year, the growth rate of monetary aggregate M2 was negative,
continuing the downward trend of 2014, the lowest growth level of
-14.2 percent was recorded in October. The both components of
the monetary aggregate M2 recorded negative evolutions. Thus,
deposits in national currency and money in circulation decreased
compared to 2014 by 7.6 percent or by MDL 1 959.8 million and
by 11.4 percent or by MDL 1 999.3 million, respectively.
The evolution of deposits in MDL recorded a pronounced growth
rate decrease, reaching a minimum of -18.1 percent in October
and it reversed its downward trend in the last months of the year,
so the average growth rate of deposits in MDL was -10.6 percent
in November-December. The evolution of money in circulation
recorded a downward trend during the year, the growth rate was
by 11.2 percentage points lower than in 2014.
At the same time, money supply (M312) decreased by 3.0 percent,
by 8.3 percentage points lower than in 2014 (Chart 3.8).
11Money supply M2 includes money in circulation (M0), deposits in MDL and
money market instruments.
12Money supply M3 includes money supply M2 and deposits in foreign currency
of residents expressed in MDL.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
43
At the end of 2015, the growth rate of deposits in foreign currency
was 8.8 percent.
The term deposits in foreign currency,
recalculated in MDL, decreased by MDL 47.9 million or by 0.3
percent annually, while sight deposits increased by MDL 2 040.3
million or by 36.9 percent.
Thus, by the end of 2015, the share of deposits in MDL was 49.0
percent (-4.1 percentage points), while the share of deposits in
foreign currency - 51.0 percent (+4.1 percentage points).
The term deposits held the largest share in the structure of deposits
in MDL as at the end of December 2014, accounting for 66.1
percent, increasing by 1.1 percentage points compared to the end
of December 2014.
The total volume of new term deposits attracted in national
currency during 2015 recorded an increase of MDL 4 361.9 million,
or by 19.6 percent. This was due to the increase in new term
deposits attracted in national currency from individuals, which
recorded an increase of MDL 5 866.6 million or by 45.6 percent
more than in 2014, simultaneously with the decrease in new
deposits attracted in national currency from legal entities by MDL
1 504.7 million compared to 2014 (-16.1 percent).
During 2015, the volume of term deposits in foreign currency,
recalculated in MDL, recorded an increase of MDL 875.7 million or
by 5.0 percent more than in 2014, as a result of the increase
in volume of new deposits attracted in foreign currency from
individuals by MDL 1 199.0 million (8.8 percent) and the decrease
in volume of new deposits attracted in foreign currency from legal
entities by MDL 323.3 million (-8.5 percent).
Lending market13
During 2015, the growth rate of the balance of credits granted
to economy accelerated its decline recorded at the end of 2014
and reversed its downward trend in the last two months of the
year, in the context where the credits granted to legal entities also
recorded a positive annual dynamic. Thus, at the end of 2015,
the annual growth rate of the total balance of credits granted to
economy increased up to 3.2 percent, while at the end of 2014,
it recorded a decrease of 3.2 percent (Chart 3.9). This increase

At the end of 2015, the balance of credits granted to legal entities,
the share of which is 85.8 percent of total balance of credits
granted by banking system, recorded a growth rate of 3.3 percent.
The positive dynamic was marked by the increase of the
component in foreign currency recalculated in national currency
by 14.7 percent, while that in national currency recorded a

granted to household slowed down during 2015 by 9.7 times,

decline recorded by the component in national currency (Chart

Evolution of new granted loans

accounting for MDL 25 424.3 million, decreasing by 51.5 percent
compared to 2014.
The demand for loans was negatively
inﬂuenced by the continued increase of interest rates in national
currency, by the national currency depreciation and a restraint
economic activity.
The annual decline was uniform for both
components, the volume of that in national currency decreased up
to MDL 15 189.7 million (Chart 3.10) and in foreign currency fall to

Interest rates
During 2015, the National Bank of Moldova continued the upward
trend of the base rate, carrying out six increases. These increases,
inﬂuenced by the inﬂationary process, marked the evolution of
average interest rate on credits and deposits attracted by banking
system in national currency, which recorded an upward trend
(Chart 3.12).
The interest rates on new credits granted in national currency
by the banking system continued to increase during 2015. The
weighted average rate on new loans granted in national currency
recorded the level of 14.06 percent or by 3.47 percentage points
more than in 2014.
This upward trend was recorded by both
categories of clients.
Therefore, the rate on loans granted to
legal entities recorded a level of 13.67 percent, increasing by
3.42 percentage points, while those granted to individuals - by
2.89 percentage points, reaching the level of 15.26 percent. The
maximum value of average interest rate on loans granted in
national currency during 2015 accounted to 15.75 percent annually
(October 2015).
The downward development of the interest rates on loans in
foreign currency was a temperate one, falling within the corridor
of 6.55 - 7.49 percent. Thus, their average decreased in January
from 7.49 percent to 6.55 percent in November (Chart 3.10). The
weighted average rate on credits granted in foreign currency in
2015

on loans granted in foreign currency was not homogeneous for
both categories of clients. The decrease of rates was inﬂuenced
only by the cost of credits granted to legal entities, the share of

Money market
Primary market of state securities
Acting as the ﬁscal agent of the state in organizing the placement
and redemption of state securities (SS) in book-entry form, the
National Bank of Moldova carried out 195 auctions of state
securities placement during the reporting year.
In the speciﬁc circumstances of 2015 and following the need to
increase the budget deﬁcit funding at the expense of domestic
sources, the Ministry of Finance made during the year a number

of changes in volumes and types of SS proposed for sale. Thus,
the Ministry of Finance increased the volume of treasury bills (T-
bills) with a maturity of 182 and 364 days, announced weekly for
placement through auctions, maintaining the same volume for
T-bills with a maturity of 91 days, while that of government bonds
(GB) with the maturity of two and three years was increased from
MDL 12.0 million - to MDL 15.0 million. The occurrence of a gap
at the beginning of May determined the need of the Ministry of
Finance to issue T-bills with a maturity of 21 days.
At the same time, the modest interest of investors for GB with a
maturity of 3 years ceased their issuance since August for
non-determined period of time.
In order to achieve the
quantitative objective of the budget deﬁcit funding by SS
issuance, beginning with the second half of November, the
Ministry of Finance complemented with MDL 69.0 million the
weekly volume announced for placement of T-bills and ceased the
T-bills issuance with a maturity of 21 days.

The impact of modifying the liquidity conditions on money market
has also affected the primary market of state securities, the ratio
of the volume of bids submitted and the volume announced for
placement during 2015 was less than one (Chart 3.16).
The volume of state securities issued during this period increased
compared to 2014 and the value accumulated of MDL 9 254.9
million was signiﬁcantly lower than the volume announced for
placement (MDL 12 215.0 million). The average maturity of 211
days of issued securities was lower than that of the previous year
(233 days), as a result of changes made in issuances’ structure.
The evolution of interest rates on the state securities market
reﬂected combined inﬂuence exercised during 2015 by the change
in liquidity conditions on money market, by the situation on the
local foreign exchange market and by the increases in monetary
policy rates. The interest rates of state securities continued the

offered by banks for deposits increased non-bank investors’
interest for SS. Thus, the amount of SS purchased by non-bank
investors has doubled compared to 2014 both in absolute terms
and as a share of total awarded volume, registering MDL 1 830.4
million or 19.8 percent, compared to MDL 909.2 million or 10.6
percent in 2014. The SS purchases by non-residents were carried
out in insigniﬁcant volumes (MDL 2.9 million).
In the issues structure by maturity of SS carried out in 2015 (Chart
3.18) and (Chart 3.19), treasury bills with a maturity of 12 months
held for the ﬁrst time the largest share - 35.48 percent, followed
by those with a maturity of 6 months with a share of 32.97 percent.
The share of government bonds decreased signiﬁcantly compared
to 2014.
The analysis of interest rates evolution on state securities
breakdown by maturity shows an increased volatility and the
deviation between them decreased in the ﬁrst decade of March,
maintaining this trend until the end of the year (Chart 3.20). At
the last placement auctions of SS in 2015, the average nominal
interest rates recorded values of 24.06, 24.79 and 26.46 percent
annually for SS with maturities of 91,
182 and 364 days
respectively, being net higher than the values recorded at the end
of 2014. On the basis of the analysis by maturity, these recorded
different increases: the interest rate of SS with maturity of 91
days recorded the highest increase (more than 4 times), while the
average rates of SS with maturity of 182 and 364 days have
doubled.
The average annual interest rate of state securities with the
maturity up to one year awarded in 2015 recorded the level of
20.14 percent annually, compared to 6.68 percent annually in
2014.
The weighted average annual nominal interest rates on state
securities traded during the last two years in auctions on primary
market, breakdown by maturity, are shown in table 3.1.
The development of interest rates of SS changed in 2015 the yields
hierarchy on ﬁnancial market designed in the previous years: the
investments in state securities was at the forefront, having higher
yield than new granted credits.
However, these exceeded at
the beginning of the year the interest rates on new credits and
remained in this position until the end of the year, distancing
signiﬁcantly from them. At the same time, the gap between the
interest rates on granted credits and attracted deposits recorded a
sensitive decrease amid the increase of the last ones (Chart 3.21).
The volume of state securities placed on the primary market
through auctions, outstanding at 31 December 2015 amounted to
MDL 5 916.5 million at nominal value (Chart 3.22). At the sale -
purchase price, these securities accounted for MDL 5 162.0 million,
increasing by MDL 243.2 million compared to the end of 2014. As
from SS in circulation at 31 December 2015, those with residual
maturity up to three months constituted 38.2 percent, from 3 to 6
months - 30.5 percent, from 6 to 12 months - 28.9 percent, from
one to two years - 2.3 percent and from two to three years - 0.1
percent.

Secondary market of state securities
In the reporting year, the increase in secondary market turnover
of SS was determined by the increasing interest of non-banking
investors to state securities. Thus, the value of the selling-buying
transactions of SS with residual maturity up to one year amounted
to MDL 144.6 million (MDL 99.2 million in 2014) and the share of
81.6 percent was held by those of bank-client type compared to
63.1 percent in 2014.

(May). Beginning with June, the transactions have ceased for four
consecutive months, being renewed in insigniﬁcant volumes in

Reference interest rate on interbank money market
CHIBOR/CHIBID reference rate curve (indicative quotations of
interest rates for the placement/attraction on the interbank market
of funds in national currency) covered the increases of monetary
policy interest rate during 2015.
CHIBOR rate curve recorded an upward trend for the whole
spectrum of maturity under the impact of the increases in
monetary policy interest rate, but this recorded a higher increase,
reaching higher levels compared to the monetary policy interest
rate.
The evolution of CHIBOR reference rates was not uniform during
the year, recording a suddenly increase at the beginning of the
year and a relative ﬂattening in the next period. This recorded
a new increase in September, reaching maximum values in the
last decade of October and maintained this position until the end
of the year. On average, the reference rates increased by about
13.0 percentage points compared to the end of 2014 and the
adjustment was relatively uniform by all maturities.
CHIBOR 2W interest rate was 22.32 percent at the end of 2015
compared to 8.54 percent at the end of 2014.

The securities book-entry system (BES) organized by the National
Bank of Moldova as a settlement system of securities issued by the
Ministry of Finance and National Bank is of systemic importance
for the functioning of securities market, ensuring the participants
in the market that the transactions are executed properly and in a
timely manner. Given that the participants report the securities
held by investors, the BES operates as an important instrument for
the integrity control of a certain issue, preventing the creation or
unjustiﬁed decrease of securities issued and playing an important
role in maintaining the investors’ conﬁdence.
The BES is involved in the provision of collateral by participants
within money market operations carried out by the National Bank
and lending standing facilities granted by the National Bank and
within the collateralisation process on interbank market.
On 31 December 2015, the securities in the total amount
registered in the book-entry system accounted for MDL 8 718.9
million at nominal value, which by issuers are as follows:

51
National Bank of Moldova (SS delivered to the NBM as a result of

The structure of SS recorded in the BES by holders, as of 31
December 2015, was modiﬁed compared to 2014, as follows:
• the share held by non-bank investors increased due to the
increase of state securities interest rate;
• the share held by the National Bank decreased and the share
held by banks increased, as a result of the decrease in balance
of SS purchased by the National Bank from banks through
repo operations.
The NBM Certiﬁcates in the total amount of MDL 617.0 million,
recorded in the BES at the face value, have been recorded in the
licensed banks portfolios.
During 2015, the aggregate value of transactions in the amount of
9 005 operations settled in the book-entry system at the National
Bank accounted for MDL 101 077.0 million, of which:
• Operations on primary market (new issues) - MDL 38 822.0
million;
• Redemption and interest payment by the Ministry of Finance
of the SS at maturity - MDL 17 364.8 million;
• Redemption of NBM Certiﬁcates at maturity by the National
Bank - MDL 20 354.9 million;
• Operations on OTC secondary market - SS selling-buying
operations - MDL 144.6 million;
• Pledging operations - MDL 2 515.2 million;
• Open market operations - MDL 21 875.5 million.
In 2015, DvP (delivery versus payment) held a share of 97.4
percent of total transfers, while FoP (free of payment) transfers -
2.6 percent, the latter being the transfers made between banks
and the NBM on securities pledging on granting intraday/overnight
credits and transfers between banks and their clients on SS selling-
buying operations on secondary market.

Foreign exchange market
Evolution of nominal and real effective exchange rate
During 2015, the ofﬁcial nominal exchange rate of the
national currency increased against the USD by 25.9 percent
and against the EUR - by 13.1 percent compared to the end of
2014 (Chart 3.31).
The average ofﬁcial nominal exchange rate of national
currency recorded the same tendency in 2015, increasing

against the USD and EUR by 34.0 percent and by 12.2 percent,
respectively, compared to the average ofﬁcial nominal exchange
rate of the national currency recorded in 2014.
In 2015, the trend of depreciation against the USD continued to
have a regional character, being also speciﬁc to currencies of
major trading partners with notable shares in external trade of the
Republic of Moldova. The Russian ruble, the Ukrainian hryvnia and
Belarusian ruble recorded more signiﬁcant depreciations against
the U.S. dollar compared to Moldovan leu (Chart 3.32).
At the same time, the real effective exchange rate of the
national currency14 depreciated insigniﬁcantly, by 0.3 percent.
In this context, Ukraine - by 1.09 percentage points, Russia -
by 0.51 percentage points, China – by 0.40 percentage points
have mainly contributed to the depreciation of the real effective
exchange rate, while the evolutions in other partner countries
have counterbalanced this dynamic (Chart 3.33 and Chart 3.34).

In 2015, the potential of the Republic of Moldova to attract foreign
exchange ﬂows continued to decrease, under the conditions of a
precarious situation in the Western and Eastern countries of the
main trading partners and of other countries from the region.
Respectively, the European Union, mainly the euro area has been
marked negatively in the ﬁrst half of the year by the lengthy
negotiations
with
the
Greek
authorities
relating
to
the
abandonment of the euro area or the implementation of the
package of austere measures imposed by the external creditors,
on the one hand, and by the persistence of the risks of deﬂation
and economic stagnation, on the other hand. At the same time,
additional social, political and economic challenges occurred amid
the refugee crisis erupted in the European Union.
The slump in oil prices on foreign markets and the extension of
economic sanctions by the EU and USA have exposed the Russian
Federation to an economic recession in 2015.
At the same time, the persistence of internal risks, mainly the
unfavourable developments in the domestic banking sector and
the vulnerable socio-political situation have determined the
increasing aversion of external ﬁnanciers towards the Republic of
Moldova. Overall, these developments formed the basis for the
decrease in foreign exchange ﬂows in the Republic of Moldova,
14Calculated based on the share of main trading partners and the average

Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
53
which resulted in a foreign exchange shortage on the domestic
foreign exchage market, implicitly conditioning the depreciation of
the national currency against the major currencies. Respectively,
in 2015:
• exports of goods15 decreased by 15.9 percent and imports16
- by 25.0 percent compared to 2014;
• net transfers of foreign currency in favour of individuals
decreased by 30.0 percent;
• net reﬂows of external credits and investments recorded USD
(-131.0)16 million.
In this context, the net supply of foreign currency from individuals
accounted for USD 1 549.8 million, decreasing by 24.3 percent
compared to 2014 and satisﬁed only 85.6 percent of the net
demand of foreign currency from the economic agents. This, in
turn, decreased by 30.8 percent, accounting for USD 1 810.4
million.
The depreciation of MDL and other currencies occurred due to the
strengthening of the USD position on external markets, as a result
of the labour market recovery and economic activity intensiﬁcation
in the USA and under the conditions of an economic slowdown
in emerging markets, namely in China and to the uncertainities
associated with the euro area developments etc.

Moldovan leu was exposed to certain pressures of depreciation in
the ﬁrst two months of 2015, when a signiﬁcant foreign currency
deﬁcit was recorded on the domestic foreign exchange market
amid the developments in the domestic banking sector. The deﬁcit
was determined by an increased demand for foreign currency, both
from legal entities and individuals, including in the context of their
preference to make savings in foreign currency than in national
currency. Respectively, during the ﬁrst two months of 2015, net
supply of foreign currency from individuals recorded decreasing
levels of MDL 90.4 million and satisﬁed only 27.1 percent of the
net demand of foreign currency from the economic agents in the
amount of USD 333.0 million (Chart 3.35). In these circumstances,
the exchange rate of national currency reached until 18 February
2015 a historical peak against the USD, increasing by 34.4 percent
compared to the level recorded at the end of 2014.
During this period, NBM intervened on the domestic foreign
exchange market by selling USD 237.3 million17
and has
undertaken some monetary policy measures to avoid an excessive
depreciation of the natioal currency, which could impose some
major risks to achieve the NBM fundamental objective.
Subsequently, the situation on domestic foreign exchange market
improved due to the measures undertaken by the NBM. Thus, net
supply of foreign currency recorded in March an increase from
individuals (USD 119.0 million), on the one hand, and a decrease
in foreign currency needs from economic agents (to USD 104.5
million), on the other hand. In these circumstances, the exchange
15NBS data.
16Preliminary data.
17The sum includes the special interventions performed by Banca de Economii
S.A., BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A.


54
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
rate of the national currency recorded a trend of ﬁrm appreciation,
which also continued during the ﬁrst week of April (of 17.0 percent
compared to the peak level recorded in February).
During the next periods, until the end of 2015, the exchange rate
of the national currency reversed its trajectory towards a gradual
depreciation and this dynamic revealed the susceptibility of the
Republic of Moldova economy to the unfavourable external climate,
the vulnerabilities in domestic banking sector and to the uncertain
socio-political situation internally.
In this context, the exchange rate of MDL against USD increased
in the second quarter of 2015 by 0.7 percent compared to the
previous quarter.
In the second quarter of 2015, the migration phenomenon of
the individuals’ savings from MDL in foreign currency has come
to an end.
In these circumstances, the net supply of foreign
currency from individuals recorded a gradual monthly increase,
being by 2.3 times higher (USD 473.6 million) compared with
the previous quarter.
At the same time, the net demand of
foreign currency from individuals (USD 446.7 million) increased
slightly - by 2.1 percent and was covered entirely by the net
supply of foreign currency (106.0 percent). Although there was

recorded a surplus of foreign currency in the second quarter of
2015, the pressures of depreciation on MDL were caused by
multiple uncertainities occurred on domestic foreign exchange
market in June, created by the resignation of the Government of
the Republic of Moldova, preelectoral period for local elections and
the decision of the NBM to establish special supervision over BC
„MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK” S.A., BC „Moldindconbank” S.A. and
B.C. „VICTORIABANK” S.A.
At the same time, MDL was exposed to some pressures of
depreciation, following the net outﬂows of external loans and
investments (USD -51.2 million) recorded in the second quarter of
2015.
In the third quarter of 2015, both the net demand of foreign
currency from economic agents and net supply of foreign currency
from individuals have increased compared to the previous quarter.
Net foreign currency sales to legal entities amounted to USD 541.8
million, increasing by 21.3 percent amid the increased demand of
foreign currency from importers of energy resources and certain
mobile telephone companies for the distribution of dividends in
favour of the founders thereof. On the other hand, the net supply of
foreign currency from individuals (USD 508.9 million) increased to a
lesser extent - by 7.5 percent. The increase in net supply of foreign
currency was, to some extent, determined by the withdrawals of
funds (USD 62.218 million) in particular in EUR, made by individuals
from their deposit accounts.
Respectively, the exchange rate of MDL increased against USD
by 5.9 percent compared to the previous quarter, due to lower
coverage of net demand of foreign currency by net supply of
foreign currency in the third quarter of 2015, up to 93.9 percent.
The extension of the gap between the net demand of foreign
currency from the economic agents and net supply of foreign

55
currency from individuals has also continued in the fourth quarter
of 2015. Respectively, the coverage of net demand of foreign
currency by net supply of foreign currency reached a level of 93.1
percent.
As a result of the economic activity decrease, in the fourth quarter
the net demand of foreign currency from legal entities decreased
signiﬁcantly, by 29.0 percent compared to the third quarter of
2015, to USD 384.5 million and about half of this amount was
requested only by the energy resources importers.
At the same time, the net supply of foreign currency from
individuals decreased - by 29.7 percent compared to the third
quarter, up to USD 357.8 million, against the background of
declining transfers in foreign currency in favour of individuals.
In these circumstances, in the last quarter of 2015, the national
currency depreciated against USD by 3.7 percent.
At the end of 2015, the ofﬁcial reserve assets accounted for USD 1
756.8 million decreasing by 18.5 percent or by USD 399.8 million
compared to the end of 2014 (Chart 3.37).
The decrease in
ofﬁcial reserve assets was due to the net sale interventions made
by the NBM (USD 308.1 million), and to the depreciation of the
components of foreign exchange reserves against the USD.

At the same time, the grants and loans offered by the main
development partners for ﬁnancing more projects (USD 119.8
million) have been transferred on the accounts of the Ministry of
Finance held at the NBM. Thus, the European Investment Bank
allocated credits in the amount of USD 38.8 million (equivalent of
EUR 32.05 million), to support the Road Sector Program and the
Wine Sector Restructuring Program. The World Bank Institutions
allocated USD 35.9 million, these ﬁnancial means were mostly
designed to support the projects: Competitive agriculture project,
Competitiveness enhancement project II, Improving Efﬁciency
SACET project.
At the same time, the entities from Romania transferred USD 10.5
million (equivalent of EUR 9.4 million), mainly in the form of grants,
while the International Fund for Agricultural Development allocated
USD 5.8 million, mainly in the form of loans.
At the end of 2015, ofﬁcial reserve assets continued to ensure a
sustainable coverage of the imports of goods and services (about
4.4 months of import19).

this period totalled the equivalent of USD 12 813.8 million20,

On the foreign exchange cash market, the turnover of the foreign
exchange bureaux of the licensed banks dropped more pronounced
by 24.2 percent, compared to that of the foreign exchange ofﬁces
which decreased by 9.6 percent.
In 2015, the USD continued to prevail in the structure of total
turnover of the transactions on foreign exchange market, although
it decreased in share by 4.9 percentage points, including by 2.7
percentage points - in favour of EUR and by 1.2 percentage points
- in favour of Russian ruble (Chart 3.40). Within the structure of
turnover of the foreign exchange cash market, the EUR held the
largest share (Table A.11).
In 2015, purchases of foreign currency by licensed banks on the
domestic foreign exchange market decreased by USD 10 050.0
million or by 62.5 percent compared to 2014 (Table A.12). This
was due to the decrease in purchases of foreign currency from the
resident banks - by USD 6 615.2 million (by 88.5 percent) and from
the NBM - by USD 1 220.3 million (by 71.7 percent).
At the same time, the purchases of foreign currency from legal
entities decreased by USD 1 353.9 million (by 39.0 percent), while
the purchases from individuals decreased less, by USD 757.6
million (by 26.4 percent).
In these circumstances, the licenced banks purchased in 2015
foreign currency on the domestic foreign exchange market, mainly
from legal entities (35.3 percent), individuals (35.2 percent),
resident banks (14.3 percent) and NBM (8.0 percent).
Compared to 2014, sales of foreign currency by licensed banks
of the Republic of Moldova on the domestic foreign exchange
market in 2015 decreased by USD 9 912.2 million or by 62.8

At the end of 2015, the share of disposable funds in foreign
currency was 33.9 percent of total balance sheet assets in foreign
currency of licensed banks, increasing slightly by 0.7 percentage
points compared to 31 December 2014. This indicator has varied

currency recorded by local banks decreased by the equivalent of

On 31 December 2015, the balance of required reserves in foreign

Required reserves represented 11.7 percent of

and ranged between 10.1 percent and 11.9 percent during the

Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
61
Management of foreign exchange reserves
Pursuant to Article 5, 16, and 53 of the Law on the National
Bank of Moldova, the NBM holds and manages foreign exchange
reserves of the State, performs foreign exchange operations using
the foreign exchange reserves and maintains them at a level
appropriate for implementing the monetary and foreign exchange
policy of the State. The role of foreign reserves is to ensure the
ﬁnancial credibility and stability of the country, honour payments
related to external obligations of the State and to cope with
unforeseen exogenous shocks.
In the management of foreign reserves, the NBM ensures a high
degree of safety and a necessary level of liquidity of investment.
NBM investment policy is prudent, seeking to optimize the
proﬁtability,
on condition that the liquidity and investment
security is ensured.
An essential element of the process of
managing foreign exchange reserves is the management of
investment risk, which is achieved by imposing constraints and
limits related to investments.
Table A.22 shows the main risks related to the management of
foreign exchange reserves and the methods used to reduce them.
Under the cooperation with the World Bank, the Strategic Asset
Allocation (SAA) is implemented since July 2013. Strategic asset
allocation involves a vision of long-term asset management aimed
at achieving the optimal level of return and risk. In the context of
the SAA, international reserves are divided in three tranches
(current, liquidity and investment) depending on the speciﬁc
objectives
and
regulations,
this
approach
ensuring
more
effectively the objective achievement.
The portfolio of securities held to maturity and investments in gold
are part of the investment tranche.
Thereafter, there will be analysed the development of the world
economy and the decisions of the monetary authorities of the USA,
European Union and Great Britain, which have had an impact on
the foreign exchange management process of the NBM.
The world economy recorded a modest increase of 3.1 percent in
2015. This result reﬂects a continuous slowdown in growth rate
of the emerging and developing economies, determined by the
decreased post-crisis levels of commodity prices, low capital ﬂows,
weak international trade and the persistence and intensiﬁcation
of volatility on ﬁnancial markets, which has overshadowed the
modest recovery of advanced economies, such as USA, Great
Britain, euro area and Japan, supported by the relaxed monetary
policy and ﬁnancial conditions improvement.
During the year, the world economy has faced a number of issues,
including geopolitical tensions, low productivity, European refugee
crisis, slowdown in China growth rate, possibility for the Federal
Reserve to increase the interest rate and debt high level, as a
result of the ﬁnancial crisis in 2008, which increased the risk of
economic vulnerability globally.
During 2015, in the USA has occurred the reduction to a maximum
of the unemployment rate and price stability.
Labour market
continued to improve, being characterised by new jobs creation,


62
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
while the unemployment rate recorded a decrease from 6.2 percent
in 2014 to 5.3 percent in 2015. The inﬂation rate was close to
zero, lower than medium-term target of 2.0 percent, reﬂecting to a
signiﬁcant extent the decrease in energy products and non-energy
import products prices.
In order to achieve the proposed objectives, the Federal Open
Market Committee decided to maintain the reference interest rate
to the level ranging from 0.0 percent and 0.25 percent, being
initially projected to revise it in June and September, subsequently
the rate was increased by 0.25 percent only on 16 December 2015.
The USA economy recorded a growth rate of 1.0 percent in the
fourth quarter of 2015, decreasing from 2.0 percent in the third
quarter and 3.9 percent in the second quarter, a modest increase
due to the strong impact of the USD appreciation. During 2015,
gross domestic product increased by 2.4 percent, recording a
similar growth rate as in 2014.
Referring to euro area, it was decided at the beginning of 2015,
within the monetary policy meeting of the European Central Bank,
to launch an extended asset purchase programme, the monthly
purchases of securities issued by public and private sector
accounted for EUR 60 billion. It was intended to carry them out by
the end of September 2016, until a sustained adjustment of
inﬂation trajectory will occur, which should be in line with the
objective of maintaining the inﬂation rate close to 2.0 percent on
medium-term.
However, investors continued to perceive the economy of euro
area countries as one extremely affected by the disinﬂationary
risks.
Thus, the interest rate of deposit facility decreased in
December by 10 basis points, up to - 0.30 percent. The interest
rate on main reﬁnancing operations and the interest rate on
marginal lending facility remain unchanged at the level of 0.05
percent and 0.30 percent, respectively. At the same time, in the
context of reassessing the adequacy of the ECB monetary policy
orientation, the ECB’s Governing Council decided to extend the
asset purchase programme at least until the end of March 2017,
to reinvest the principal amount related to securities to maturity
from the asset purchase program as long as this is necessary and
to include the marketable credit instruments expressed in EUR and
issued by regional and domestic authorities of the euro area in the
list of eligible assets for periodical purchases carried out by the
respective national central banks.
In Great Britain, the Monetary Policy Committee set the objective
to achieve the inﬂation rate level on medium term of 2.0 percent,
so that to support the economic growth and employment. In order
to achieve the set objective, the reference rate was maintained at
the level of 0.5 percent and the volume of purchases of assets at
the level of Pound Sterling 375 billion during the reporting year.
The inﬂation rate was of around 0.0 percent, in spite of the
measures undertaken during 2015. About three quarters of the
inﬂation rate deviation from the target level reﬂect the low prices
of energy products, food and other imported goods. The latter
quarter reﬂects the previous risks of domestic costs increase,
particularly the labour cost. In addition, core inﬂation maintained
the low level, as a result of the Pound Sterling appreciation, which
has a continuous impact on imported goods prices.

quarter of 2015 the lowest growth rate over the last two and a
half years.

The ofﬁcial reserve assets decreased on 31 December 2015 by
18.54 percent compared to 31 December 2014 or by about USD
399.83 million (from USD 2 156.63 million to USD 1 756.81 million).
The interventions on the foreign exchange interbank market in the
form of foreign currency sales and the foreign exchange rates
depreciation of the main component currencies are the main
factors that inﬂuenced the decrease of ofﬁcial reserve assets.
As for the management of foreign exchange reserves,the NBM
invests
in
safe
instruments
used
by
other
central
banks:
placements on correspondent accounts (usually with other central
banks), term placements in foreign currency and securities, which
are
classiﬁed
into
supranational
securities
(issued
by
supranational institutions), state securities (issued by the U.S.
government, EU member governments, other governmental
issuers with high credit rating, with State explicit guarantee) and
non-government securities (issued by high rating agencies).
Some of the foreign exchange reserves are managed externally
by the World Bank. According to the Investment Consulting and
Management Agreement, concluded on 8 December 2010 between
the International Bank for Reconstruction and Development (IBRD)
and the National Bank of Moldova (NBM), the IBRD became the
consultant and the agent of the NBM for managing a part of the
external assets, limited to 20 percent of ofﬁcial reserve assets
(the share of externally managed assets was 11.74 percent of
the ofﬁcial reserve assets as of 31 December 2015). Taking into
account the importance and effectiveness of the collaboration with
the World Bank in the management of foreign exchange reserves,
the agreement was extended on 24 January 2014 for a period of
two years.
During 2015, the portfolio of securities held to maturity was not
supplied with new securities. The value of this portfolio at 31
December 2015 amounted to USD 471.18 million or 26.82 percent
of ofﬁcial reserve assets.
In accordance with the Decision of the Council of Administration of
the NBM no.286 of 23 December 2014, effective as of 1 January
2015, the maximum limit of securities portfolio held to maturity
increased from 20.0 percent to 30.0 percent of ofﬁcial reserve
assets.
The evolution of foreign exchange reserves during 2013 - 2015,
divided by investment instruments, is shown in chart 3.44.
It
should be mentioned the increasing tendency of placements on

state securities held in portfolio recorded the level of 23.71 percent

State’s deposits placed with the National Bank of Moldova
Under the Agreement of the National Bank of Moldova and the
Ministry of Finance of 5 December 2007, the object of which is the
rendering of services by the NBM to the Ministry of Finance, and
based on the provisions of the Regulation on accepting deposits
from the Ministry of Finance, approved by the Decision of the
Council of Administration of the NBM no.358 of 28 December 2006,
the Ministry of Finance placed in 2015 term deposits in MDL with
the National Bank of Moldova, with the maturity from 43 days to
181 days, in the total amount of MDL 31.0 million.
Compared to 2014, the amount of deposits placed with the
National Bank decreased by MDL 339.5 million during the
reporting year.
The deposits were placed at the NBM base rate, which was variable
over the duration of the deposit agreement.
As at 31 December 2015, the balance of term deposits of the
Ministry of Finance was null.
3.6
Supervision of the banks’ activities
General information
Taking into account the systemic ﬁnancial crisis situation, the need
to ensure ﬁnancial stability and protect the interests of depositors,
the National Committee for Financial Stability agreed at its meeting
of 05 August 2015 on the withdrawal of the license of Banca de
Economii S.A., BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A.
At the same time, in order to avoid the disturbance of the ﬁnancial
stability of the banking system or the activity of public institutions,
the Government of the Republic of Moldova, the National Bank of
Moldova and some authorities of central public administration and
public institutions concluded the Memorandum of understanding
"On the measures to be taken to withdraw the licences of Banca
de Economii S.A., BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. „UNIBANK”
S.A. and the time-limits for their achievement".
In accordance with the provisions of the Memorandum, each
authority performed a number of actions relating to the transfer
of the treasury accounts and other accounts of the state budget,
social payments and other compensations, and the transfer of the
accounts of individuals and legal entities from the aforementioned
banks to other banks. Thus, Banca de Economii S.A., BC „BANCA
SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A., with the National Bank
assistance, carried out on 12-15 October 2015 the transfer of the
accounts of individuals and legal entities in other banks. As at 30


66
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
September 2015, the funds of depositors and economic agents
transferred in other commercial banks recorded the level of MDL
737.3 million or 44.5 percent of the total deposits of legal entities
and individuals.
The Executive Board of the National Bank of Moldova withdrawn
on 16 October 2015 the licence for ﬁnancial activity of Banca
de Economii S.A., BC "BANCA BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C.
„UNIBANK” S.A. as a result of their insolvency and other violations
of the legislation in force and appointed the liquidators for the
forced liquidation of the banks.
It should be mentioned that since the withdrawal of the licences of
Banca de Economii S.A., BC "BANCA BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C.
„UNIBANK” S.A. until 31 December 2015, these banks collected
ﬁnancial means (from loans payment, sale of pledge, sale of assets,
other receipts) in the total amount of MDL 326.5 million, including:
Banca de Economii S.A. – MDL 153.3 million; BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A”
S.A. – MDL 96.1 million; B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A. – MDL 77.1 million. At
the same time, since the withdrawal of licences until 31 December
2015, these banks reimbursed the debts related to emergency
loans granted prior to the liquidators’ appointment in the total
amount of MDL 441.4 million, of which: Banca de Economii S.A. –
MDL 74.8 million; BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. – MDL 121.8 million;
B.C. „UNIBANK” S.A. – MDL 244.7 million.
As at 31 December 2015, the banking sector of the Republic
of Moldova included 11 banks licensed by the National Bank of
Moldova, of which 4 are branches of foreign banks and ﬁnancial
groups.
In accordance with the provisions of the Law on Financial
Institutions, the National Bank of Moldova initiated a special
supervision procedure for a period of three months to three banks
(BC „Moldindconbank” S.A., B.C. „VICTORIABANK” S.A. and BC
„MOLDOVA - AGROINDBANK” S.A., which hold in total 66.7 percent
of the banking sector assets) of those 11 banks licensed by the
NBM, following the detection of issues related to non-transparent
shareholder structure, engaging in high risk lending operations. At
the same time, National Bank of Moldova, by its decision, has
prescribed a diagnostic study for these banks, which shall be
conducted by a company specialized in this ﬁeld.
It should be mentioned that on 12 September 2015 and
subsequently on 14 December 2015, National Bank continued for
another three months the special supervision procedure, as a
result
of
the
unsolved
issues
related
to
transparency
of
shareholder structure and engaging in high risk operations.
The total number of banks’ subdivisions was 814, of which 289
branches and 525 agencies.
In 2015, there were opened 10
branches, 207 agencies and there were closed 70 branches, 672
agencies, due mainly to the licence withdrawal of 3 banks.
The total number of staff employed in the banking sector as of 31
December 2015 amounted to 7 621 employees. On average, each
employee in the banking sector accounted for MDL 9.1 million
assets as of 31 December 2015, increasing by MDL 0.6 million (7.1
percent) compared to the end of 2014.

1.3 percentage points compared to the end of 2014. The medium

Generale” S.A., B.C. „EXIMBANK-Gruppo Veneto Banca” S.A., B.C.
„ProCredit Bank” S.A., „FinComBank" S.A., B.C. „ENERGBANK"

The small banks group included 3 banks (BCR Chi¸sin˘au S.A., BC

at the end of 2015 remained the same as at the end of 2014.
BC „MOLDOVA - AGROINDBANK" S.A. held the largest share in the
total assets of the bank against the total assets of the banking
sector,
representing
26.3
percent,
followed
by
BC
„Moldindconbank” S.A. – 22.6 percent.
As a result of the analysis of the banking sector concentration
of the Republic of Moldova, there was calculated the Herﬁndahl-
Hirschmann index (value of which does not exceed 10 thousand),
based on the bank’s share of assets to total assets in banking
sector. This indicator is calculated by summing the squares of
the market shares of all the banks in the sector. The international
practice uses the following division:
• HHI less than 100 indicates a market with perfect competition;
• HHI between 100 and 1500 indicates an unconcentrated
market;
• HHI between 1500 and 2500 indicates a moderate market;
• HHI above 2500 indicates a highly concentrated market.
28Except for BC „BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A., Banca de Economii S.A. and B.C.
„UNIBANK” S.A.


68
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
Thus, based on the calculations made, obtaining HHI=1 734, it
is concluded that the banking sector of the Republic of Moldova
presents a moderate concentration.
Table A.23 shows the structure of banking sector assets by main
components.
In 2015, within the structure of assets, there have been recorded
the following increases (in descending order):
• cash and cash equivalents29 (mainly due to the increase of
"Nostro" accounts in the NBM) - by MDL 7 239.4 million (50.7
percent);
• loans and receivables - by MDL 1 933.0 million (5.0 percent);
• tangible assets - by MDL 106.5 million (5.9 percent);
• claims on taxes - by MDL 41.8 million (234.0 percent);
• intangible assets - by MDL 21.4 million (8.4 percent);
• investments held to maturity - by MDL 13.3 million (0.4
percent).
At the same time, the followings have decreased:
• ﬁnancial assets held-for-trading - by MDL 125.4 million (80.8
percent);
• other assets - by MDL 92.6 million (13.8 percent);
• ﬁnancial assets available-for-sale - by MDL 14.1 million (3.4
percent);
• ﬁxed assets and disposal groups classiﬁed as held for sale -
by MDL 6.3 million (2.6 percent).
The maximum share in total assets is held by loans and receivables
- 58.4 percent, by 5.6 percentage points less compared to the end
of 2014.
Cash and cash equivalents constitited 31.2 percent,
increasing by 7.4 percentage points. Investments held to maturity
had a share of 5.4 percent, decreasing by 0.8 percentage points
and tangible assets - 2.8 percent, decreasing by 0.2 percentage
points compared to the end of 2014. Other items held insigniﬁcant
shares in total assets.

Capital of banks
Tier I Capital is a part of the total regulatory capital for which
the minimum required amount for performing ﬁnancial activities is
established as in accordance with Article 26 of the Law on Financial
Institutions.
Tier I on the banking system reached the level of MDL 8 995.7
million, increasing by MDL 1 384.9 million (18.2 percent) compared
to the end of 2014.
Tier I capital increase was driven mainly by the proﬁt in the
amount of MDL 1 442.7 obtained during 2015 and by the decrease
of the amount calculated but unreserved of the allowances for
impairment losses on assets and conditional commitments by MDL
51.0 million (2.4 percent). At the same time, net intangible assets
increased by MDL 20.8 million (8.2 percent). The ordinary shares
and the capital surplus have also increased by MDL 2.4 million
and MDL 53.9 million, due to the repurchase in January 2015 of
the own shareholdings by „FinComBank” S.A. in the amount of
MDL 10.4 million with the price of MDL 287.0 per share and due to

the realisation in April of treasury bills in the amount of MDL 12.9
million by BC „MOLDOVA - AGROINDBANK” S.A. Both transactions
were carried out at a higher price than the shares’ nominal value.

amount of MDL 164.0 million.
As at 31 December 2015, the Tier I capital of all banks complied
with the minimum level of required capital (minimum required
level is ≥MDL 200 million).

capital is limited to maximum 100.0 percent of Tier I capital) minus
shareholdings in the capital of other banks holding the license of
the National Bank of Moldova.

Out of total number of banks, 4 banks have their capital formed

In accordance with the provisions of the Law on Financial
Institutions, the National Bank is permanently tracing the changes
in the banks’ ownership structure, in order to ensure the certainty
in identiﬁng the beneﬁcial owners of qualifying holdings by
achieving the following:


72
Annual Report (NBM, 2015)
• the establishment of requirements for submitting by banks
reports on direct/indirect holders, including the beneﬁcial
owners of qualifying holdings in the bank’s share capital;
• permanent follow-up of the transactions with banks’ shares;
• request for submitting information related to the identity and
activity conducted by the potential acquirers, including the
shareholders and beneﬁcial owners, as well the submission of
questionnaires, in some cases of other documents necessary
to assess their quality;
• performance of investigations related to the manner of
acquisition of bank’s shares and request for competent
authorities’ assistance.
As a result of the continuing monitoring of the banks’ ownership
structure, the Executive Board of the National Bank of Moldova
found
that
a
goup
of
four
shareholders
of
BC
„MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK” S.A. are acting in concert and acquired
a qualifying holding in the bank’s share capital in the amount of
3.53 percent, without prior written permission from the National
Bank.
The
aforementioned
shareholders
of
BC
„MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK” S.A. were informed of the provisions of
the Law on Financial Institutions regarding the rights suspension:
voting rights,
right of convocation and deployment of the
shareholders’ general meeting, right to include metters on the
agenda, right to propose candidates for members of the bank’s
council and the right to receive dividends. Thus, the shareholders
are obliged within three months from the date of 23 December
2015 to dispose the shares related to qualifying holding acquired
in the share capital of BC „MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK” S.A. without
prior permission from the National Bank.
Liabilities of banks
As at 31 December 2015, the liabilities of banks accounted for MDL
57 330.0 million, increasing by MDL 7 727.8 million (15.6 percent)
as compared to 31 December 2014. Financial liabilities measured
at amortized cost (customers’ deposits, bonds issued by banks,
other loans, subordinated debts, overnight loans, securities sold
under repo) held the largest share in total liabilities – 98.9 percent
(MDL 56 712.0 million), by MDL 7 779.1 million (15.9 percent) more
than at the end of 2014. Customers’ deposits accounted for MDL
50 565.6 million or 88.2 percent of total liabilities and the absolute
value of customers’ deposits increased by MDL 7 536.3 million
(17.5 percent) compared to 31 December 2014.
At the same
time, other liabilities (overdraft on “Nostro” accounts, overnight
loans from the NBM, overnight loans from banks, loans from
banks) increased by MDL 116.2 million (43.2 percent), accounting
for MDL 385.1 million, provisions - by MDL 24.0 million (31.4
percent), accounting for MDL 100.5 million (provisions for business
restructuring, liabilities related to employee beneﬁts, provisions
related to damage costs arising from lawsuits against the bank,
funding commitments and other provisions), ﬁnancial liabilities
held for trading - by MDL 0.6 million (100.0 percent).
At the
same time, the tax liabilities decreased by MDL 192.0 million (59.2
percent), accounting for MDL 132.3 million (Table A.24).

Liquidity of banks
Long-term liquidity ratio of the banking system (liquidity principle
I, assets with terms more than two years/ﬁnancial resources with
potential withdrawal term of over two years) recorded a level of
0.7 (average per sector). This indicator has been complied with by
all banks during the reporting period (limit for each bank ≤1).
The current liquidity per sector - liquidity principle II (liquid assets
expressed in cash, deposits with the NBM, liquid securities, and net
interbank funds with terms up to one month/total assets x100%)
equalled to 41.5 percent, increasing by 8.6 percentage points
during 2015. This indicator has been complied with by all banks
(limit for each bank ≥20 percent).
Thus, the liquidity indicator reveals the existence of the adequate
sources of funding to cover the potential needs both on short and
long-term.

due to the increase of impairment of ﬁnancial assets not at fair

banking sector indicates the banks’ ability to generate income in
the future.
In order to monitor the banking risks, the National Bank of Moldova
has developed stress tests, which are supervising tools used to
assess the strong points of each bank and the banking sector
resistance to shocks. Stress tests used by the National Bank are
focused on credit risk, liquidity risk, interest rate risk and exchange
rate risk and the combinations thereof. All stress tests are drawn
up based on data reported by licensed banks and are applied in
the whole banking sector, on groups of banks and individual banks.
Following the stress tests carried out, it was found that the banking
sector is able to withstand simulated shocks, except for severe
shocks (historical highs) on credit risk affecting the solvency
indicator of some individual banks, which reduce under the limits
regulated by the National Bank.
However, the probability to
materialise such scenarios is very low and the banks undertake
necessary measures to improve the quality of loan portfolio, which
will allow to reduce the losses in case of stress scenario.
With regard to liquidity risk, the stress testing results conﬁrm the
liquidity high level of the banking sector, banks being able to
withstand deposit withdrawals at maximum historical rates, while
liquidity deﬁcits, for the stress testing scenarios applied, were not
recorded.
At the same time, the National Bank is conducting a study on
identifying the banking sector vulnerability in order to assess the
