potential risks that could affect the stability of each bank and of
the sector regarding the capital categories, assets quality, income,
liquidity and sensitivity to market risk. The study results indicate
the vulnerability low level of the banking system as at the end of
2015, with small vulnerabilities in terms of sensitivity to market
risk and proﬁtability.
3.7
Strategic Plan of the National Bank
of Moldova for 2013-2017
To fulﬁl its role, functions and activities so that to serve the best
interests of Moldovan citizens, the National Bank of Moldova
approved
a
Strategic
Plan
that
provide
the
direction
of
development over the next ﬁve years. The Strategic Plan of the
National Bank of Moldova is approved for 2013-2017.
The Strategic Plan is intended to strengthen the NBM’s role in
society by fulﬁlling all tasks undertaken, ensuring an efﬁcient use
of resources and reducing the exposure to risks. Its priorities set
through strategic objectives are aimed to ensure a high level of
efﬁciency, transparency and performance, by aligning to the best
international practices of communication, credibility and corporate
governance.
The Strategic Plan includes several key elements such as: mission,
vision, values, basic ﬁelds of activity, strategic objectives etc.
Mission of the National Bank of Moldova
The primary objective of the National Bank of Moldova shall be
to ensure and maintain price stability. Without prejudice to the
primary objective, the National Bank of Moldova shall promote and
maintain a ﬁnancial system based on market principles and shall
support the general economic policy of the state.
Vision of the National Bank of Moldova
The National Bank of Moldova is an independent public authority,
efﬁcient and credible, which promotes an adequate monetary
policy and helps ensuring the integrity and stability of the ﬁnancial
system, consistently applying the best international practices in
the interest of society.
Values of the National Bank of Moldova
Civic engagement – activities oriented towards the beneﬁt of
public interest;
Efﬁciency – application of innovation and of modern technologies
in resources usage;
Excellency – demonstration of competence and professionalism
in exercising and adopting the best international practices;
Transparency
–
transparent
and
impartial
attitude
in
the
decision-making process;
Accountability
–
responsible
attitude
toward
society
in
implementing the assigned tasks;
Integrity – ethical behaviour at bank and each employee level;


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
77
Credibility
–
conduct
that
inspires
and
maintains
public
conﬁdence.
By establishing the basic ﬁelds of activity, the speciﬁc functions of
the NBM are highlighted, these ﬁelds of activity actually reﬂecting
the NBM’s mission and constituting its basic pillars. The basic
ﬁelds of activity are a source of formulation of the strategic goals:
Monetary policy – application of monetary policy instruments and
measures to ensure and maintain the price stability;
Currency issuance – supporting the economy with national
currency and promoting the national currency’s credibility;
Supervision of ﬁnancial institution – regulation and supervision of
ﬁnancial institutions’ activity;
Payment system – promotion of efﬁciency, safety, accessibility
and innovative nature of payments.
The strategic objectives are the NBM priorities set based on its
mission, for a deﬁnite period of time, in order to help achieve its
vision.
For the third consecutive year, since the Strategic Plan has been
implemented, the NBM has continued to further strive to achieve
its strategic objectives. The most important achievements in 2015
related to the NBM strategic objectives are as follows:


 
 
  
  
Strategic 
objectives 
Major achievements in 2015 
 
1. Ensure price 
stability 
In order to support the monetary policy decisions and the capacities of macroeconomic analysis 
and forecast of the national economy and inflationary process, a number of forecasts and 
researches have been made during 2015, such as: there were carried out researches on 
interference of domestic and international food prices in the economy of the Republic of 
Moldova, on identification and interpretation of business cycles, on operative indicator for the 
Republic of Moldova economic growth; the model of monetary aggregates forecasting was 
drawn up as pillar for the central forecasting model; the retail interest rates extension was 
tested within the central forecasting model; the estimation model of foreign currency and 
other sectors' deposits was drawn up.   
2. Increase the 
credibility of the 
national currency 
As from 12 October 2015, the National Bank of Moldova puts into circulation seven newly 
upgraded banknotes with the face value of MDL 1, MDL 10, MDL 20, MDL 50, MDL 100, MDL 200 
and MDL 500, which contain new security features, in order to reduce the risks of falsification 
and counterfeiting the national currency. The new security features improve the quality of the 
national currency and enhance their credibility. 
In this regard, there were organized workshops for the cashiers of the NBM and licensed banks, 
and for the cashiers of the economic agents operating in the field of retail trade, which covered 
the aspects related to security features of the modernized banknotes issued. 
3. Develop the 
supervision 
function of the 
NBM 
The Twinning project for strengthening the NBM's capacity in the field of banking regulation 
and supervision in the context of implementation of Basel II/III (partly) has been initiated, 
being achieved the followings: evaluation of the existing legal/regulatory framework related to 
the regulation of bank capital and identification of differences against the requirements of 
Basel II/III; organisation of training for NBM employees and licensed banks; drawing up 
questionnaires to assess the quantitative and qualitative impact on domestic banking sector; 
developing the implementation strategy of Basel standards through the EU legislation.  
The NBM organisational structure has been changed, providing the segregation of functions of 
the banking regulation and authorisation of that of banking supervision.  
A project on Credit Risk Register has been initiated, which aims to provide sufficient 
operational information to the National Bank of Moldova and will contribute to: optimize the 
prudential regulation and strengthen the banking supervision within a macro prudential 
framework of banks’ lending supervision, in accordance with modern principles of credit risk 
management, evaluate the banks’ ability to overcome financial stress events, and help tracking 
bank solvency and macroeconomic stability. The project is ongoing and reached the 
implementation and development phase of the information solution. 
The Memorandum of understanding between the National Bank of Moldova and banking 
supervisory authority of Latvia concerning their cooperation in the field of banking supervision 
has been signed.  
78
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)


 
 
  
  
 
4. Promote 
cashless payments 
and reduce cash in 
circulation 
The IBAN code (International Bank Account Number) for local transfers beginning with 1 
January 2016 has been implemented, which created the premises to minimise the errors, data 
processing time and to reduce transfer services costs. 
There has been organised a number of seminars/events for informing about the cashless 
instruments use in order to increase financial literacy of payment services/instruments users.  
The statistical report on payment instruments use has been drawn up and published on the 
NBM official website. 
5. Strengthen the 
financial stability 
function 
The Report on financial stability for 2014 has been approved by the NBM Executive Board.  
 
6. Improve 
external 
communication 
The new version of the NBM official website has been lunched, which includes the "Interactive 
database" mechanism - a modern and efficient tool to obtain dynamic reports and sets of 
statistical data. The website modernization aimed at rendering more effective the NBM 
communication with external parties and ensuring to general public a more qualitative access 
to accurate information. The added value of new website is shown by: the focus on the NBM 
objectives, presentation of data in infographic regime, implementation of the information 
prioritisation mechanism, provision of the option for page content personalisation, integration 
with social networks etc.  
A consistent policy of informing the public on matters related to the NBM duties has been 
supported and promoted, in particular the systematic and timely information concerning the 
monetary policy decisions and actions involving financial stability. 
The NBM has hosted the "International Financial Education Week" (Global Money Week), 
carried out under the slogan "Save today, Safe tomorrow". 
There were organised conferences for mass-media representatives, meetings with pupils, high 
school students and students, where there were discussed subjects of interest to participants, 
such as: the history of Moldovan leu introduction in circulation, national currency 
modernisation, the NBM monetary policy etc. 
7. Strengthen the 
NBM image and 
credibility  
 
 
 
It was promoted the strategy of informing the society about the principles, determinants and 
interdependencies of the inflationary process, other tasks of the NBM and macroeconomic 
phenomena underlying the decisions and actions of the NBM.  
It was provided systematically information on financial stability measures.  
NBM organised the Doors Open Day, giving the public the opportunity to visit the exhibition 
hall of the bank and get more information about the banknotes and coins in circulation since 
the establishment of the NBM until now. 
 
 
Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
79


 
 
  
  
8. Improve the 
NBM operational 
activities efficiency
In order to optimise the cash processing activity and ensure a higher quality level of cash in 
circulation, there was totally automated the cash processing/packing activity, including the 
shredding of cash unfit for circulation. 
The NBM' activity processes management system has been developed through the 
development of a new legislative framework based on recognised standards in the field, 
identification of all the NBM activity processes and the creation of a database. 
The projects management function has been developed through the implementation of the 
central register service of projects: planning, documentation, reporting, quality assurance, with 
related software.  
The implementation of the Bloomberg single trading platform for open market operations has 
continued, being applied, through this platform, the method of calculation and distribution of 
the SS market prices eligible for liquidity-providing operations. 
The NBM activity processes have been optimized through the implementation of certain 
medium-term opportunities, having as impact the increase in efficiency of the use of bank's 
internal resources through: the automation and reduction of processes' duration, the decision-
making acceleration and documents' circuit optimization also through the electronic signature 
implementation. 
The information technologies function has been developed through the modernisation of 
virtual infrastructure, migration of network services (storing, printing, remote authentication, 
resources addressing service) on a new modern, mature and functionally developed platform. 
9. Streamline the 
human resources 
management 
The bank's organizational structure (II phase) has been adjusted, achieving synergies by 
grouping similar activities, segregation of functions and simplification of hierarchical reporting 
structures. Thus, the functions of banking regulation and supervision have been segregated 
and the similar activities from other subdivisions have been take over, the External relations 
and European integration service has been separated, the functions of reporting and 
regulation have been merged, the functions of foreign exchange operations and open market 
operations have been unified.  
The first cycle of the performance management system of NBM's employees has been 
implemented. 
10. Develop the 
corporate 
governance 
In November 2015, the NBM hosted the Anti-Fraud Week with a series of activities aimed at 
enhancing the awareness of fraud risk, in order to strengthen the measures for combating it. 
Several actions have been taken during the year, designed to develop the team spirit and 
increase the employee retention capacity. 
 
80
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)

81
3.8
Payment system
Oversight of payment services providers
In accordance with the Law on payment services and electronic
money no. 114 of 18 May 2012, the oversight of banks as payment
service providers and electronic money issuers is one of the NBM
duties as the supervisory authority.
In this regard, there have been carried out in 2015 on-site
inspections at licensed banks as payment service providers, at
payment companies, postal service providers and electronic
money issuers in order to verify their activity compliance with the
provisions of normative acts in force. Following the infringements
or shortcomings detected during the inspections carried out, the
NBM submitted proposals and recommendations to undertake the
necessary actions for their removal, including the application of
remedial measures and sanctions.
At the same time, pursuant to the Law no. 114 of 18 May 2012,
the NBM carries out off-site inspections of the activity performed
by payment service providers.
Oversight of payment systems
In accordance with Law on the National Bank of Moldova no. 548-
XIII of 21 July 1995, the oversight of the payment system of the
Republic of Moldova is one of the basic tasks of the National Bank
(NBM).
This task is carried out in accordance with the best international
practices, recommendations, principles, standards in this ﬁeld
issued by the Bank for International Settlements, European Central
Bank, International Monetary Fund, World Bank etc., and normative
acts issued by NBM in the payment system ﬁeld.
According to the Policy on payment system oversight in the
Republic of Moldova (approved by the Decision of the Council of
Administration of the NBM, no.143 of 30 June 2011), the National
Bank oversees payment and settlement systems, efﬁcient and
stable operation of which is essential for ﬁnancial stability and
monetary and foreign exchange policy implementation, as well as
risk management mechanisms established within the systems
widely used by the population for payments and transfers. At the
same time, the National Bank carries out oversight of cashless
payment instruments and systems of remote banking service,
which are component parts of the national payment system, in
order to ensure efﬁciency and safety of their use.
In the ﬁeld of payment and settlement systems, the followings are
subject to oversight:
• automated interbank payment system - the system through
which the interbank payments are made in MDL in the
Republic of Moldova, being managed by the National Bank;


82
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
• securities book-entry system - a depository and settlement
system of securities held and managed by the National Bank;
• ﬁnancial
risk
management
mechanisms
related
to
settlements in MDL established in the card payment systems;
• ﬁnancial and operational risk management mechanisms
established in money remittance systems.
The payment and settlement systems oversight performed by the
National Bank of Moldova includes: monitoring of systems/risk
management mechanisms; regular assessment of compliance of
systems/risk management mechanisms with the requirements set
out in the normative acts and/or applicable international standards;
where appropriate, depending on the results of the oversight,
imposition of sanctions and/or remedial measures in the form
of recommendations or binding provisions implementation with
deadlines for compliance with the legislation in force.
Oversight of cashless payment instruments and systems of remote
banking service cover: collecting data on the safety and efﬁciency
of cashless payment instruments and systems of remote banking;
monitoring any new technologies in the ﬁeld; efﬁciency and safety
analysis of indicators based on information and data obtained from
both the banks and as a result of their own research to identify the
current situation and recent developments in the use of cashless
payment instruments/systems for remote banking, their safety
and the measures that can be taken to improve the mentioned
indicators.
At the same time, in case of ﬁnding deteriorating indicators related
to safety and efﬁciency of payment instruments/remote banking
systems, the National Bank intervenes by adjusting the regulatory
framework, making recommendations or taking other appropriate
measures to enhance their soundness.
Automated Interbank Payment System
Statistical data
The Automated Interbank Payment System (AIPS) is composed
of the real time gross settlement system (RTGS) and designated-
time net settlement system (DNS). The RTGS system is designed
for processing large-value and urgent payments, while the DNS
system is designed for processing low-value payments.
In 2015, the payments processed through AIPS declined by 3.3
percent compared to 2014 and their total value by 5.4 percent.
The number of payments stood at 13.2 million and their value
exceeded MDL 679.7 billion.
In terms of payments structure, the budgetary credit transfers
hold the largest share (52.8 percent of the total number of
payments), followed by regular transfers of clients (45.4 percent)
and other types of payments (1.8 percent). In terms of value, the
structure of the payments changed: the value of regular transfers
of clients (22.4 percent of the total value of payments) exceeded
the budgetary credit transfers (17.0 percent), while the transfers
by banks in the name and on their own account held the largest
share (60.3 percent).


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
83
In 2015, the distribution of payments number per AIPS component
systems was the following: 8.4 percent for RTGS and 91.6 for DNS.
The share of transactions in the two systems component of AIPS
depends on the value of settled payments. There were settled in
RTGS system 92.7 percent of the total value of payments and only
7.3 percent in DNS system.
In 2015, on average per day34, there were settled through the
RTGS system 4.4 thousand of payments amounting to MDL 2.5
billion, and 48.0 thousand of payments through the DNS system in
the amount of MDL 198.2 million. The average value of a payment
settled in the RTGS system amounted to MDL 567.4 thousand and
a payment settled in the DNS system - MDL 4.1 thousand.
Table A.25 presents the evolution of payments settled through the
AIPS compared with 2014.
At the end of 2015, there were registered 18 participants in AIPS:
• National Bank of Moldova;
• 11 licensed banks of the National Bank of Moldova;
• Settlement Centre from Tiraspol;
• State Treasury of the Ministry of Finance;
• 2 banks under liquidation process, under the conditions laid
down in the Regulation on automated interbank payments
system;
• National Securities Depository (NSD) of the Republic of
Moldova;
• Electronic Government Centre, as mandated participant,
which has not an account in AIPS and participates in AIPS
based on the agreement concluded with the National Bank.
As the AIPS administrator, during 2015, the National Bank of
Moldova continually monitored the activity of the participants in
the system and tested their ability to respond to incidents.
Following the testing, it was found that the participants acted
properly, without inducing risks in the system and have the
necessary capacity to react promptly and effectively in incident
situations.
AIPS oversight
The AIPS oversight is carried out to ensure stable and efﬁcient
operation of this system. During 2015, the system’s availability
for its participants was 99.9 percent and was within the limits set
for the system parameter, while major incidents that disrupt the
activity of AIPS and affect its participants did not occur.
State securities book-entry system
The book-entry system of state securities (BES) is a depository and
settlement system for securities issued by the Ministry of Finance
of the Republic of Moldova on behalf of the Government of the
Republic of Moldova and by the National Bank of Moldova, the
34The term “day” means operating day.


84
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
ﬁnal settlement of operations carried out in AIPS being achieved
through real time gross settlement system (RTGS), under DvP
principle (delivery versus payment/transfer of ownership). The
BES is organized and managed by the National Bank.
The BES oversight is carried out to ensure stable and efﬁcient
operation of the system. During 2015, the system’s availability for
its participants was 100.0 percent and incidents that disrupt the
activity of the system and participants did not occur.
Cashless payment instruments
The cashless payment instruments are essential components of
the national payment system and the National Bank performs its
oversight in order to ensure efﬁciency and safety of their use.
The payment cards are the most used by the population of all
payment instruments, the remaining payment instruments being
at the initial stage of use. It should be mentioned that the NBM
takes signiﬁcant measures agreed with other authorities and
international institutions in order to promote cashless payment
instruments and to combat tax evasion.
Payment cards
The number of cards35 in circulation decreased at the end of 2015
by 0.9 percent compared to the end of 2014, accounting for 1
290 164 unities. The decrease was determined by the withdrawal
from circulation of a signiﬁcant number (324 983 unities) of cards
issued by the banks, the licences of which have been withdrawn in
the third quarter of 2015. However, the number of active cards36

The share of cashless payments for the cards issued in the Republic
of Moldova and performed locally accounted for 6.4 percent of total
value (5.6 percent for 2014) and 24.6 percent of their number (19.5

number of local operations were cashless payments performed

The number of operations with cards issued in the Republic of
Moldova and made abroad during 2015 recorded an increase of

94.0 percent (92.0 percent in 2014) of total number of operations
performed abroad (2 779 309 operations), increasing by 33.4

It should be mentioned that the

half of operations performed abroad with the cards issued in the
Republic of Moldova are "on-line" shopping.
The positive trends that were seen on the cards market in the
Republic of Moldova during 2015 were:
• given that the number of payment cards recorded a decrease
at the end of 2015, the number and value of operations,
mainly of cashless payments, performed with the cards issued
in the Republic of Moldova continued to increase;
• cash operations during e-commerce represent 61.7 percent
of operations performed abroad with payment cards issued
by the Republic of Moldova, which shows the upward trend
of these types of operations and a high level of trust of the
holders in this payment instrument;
• the acceptance network of payment cards in the Republic of
Moldova has migrated to EMV38 standards in the proportion
of 99.6 percent, while the payment cards in the proportion of
82.7 percent.
The basic indicator applied to assess the situation of payment
card fraud is the share of the total amount of fraud committed
in the total value of transactions with payment cards issued by
local licensed banks conducted both at home and abroad. In the
Republic of Moldova, this indicator amounted to 0.0071 percent
for 2014 and 0.012 percent for 2015, which is below the European
level.

87

The national public information campaign on the beneﬁts of
cashless payment instruments
In accordance with the Strategic Plan of the National Bank of
Moldova for 2013-2017, National Bank has as objective to promote
cashless payments and to reduce cash in circulation.
In this regard, one of the actions taken by the National Bank was
the ﬁrst national campaign of ﬁnancial inclusion in the Republic of
Moldova conducted in December 2014 - June 2015 with ﬁnancial
support of the EFSE Development Facility, in terms of cashless
payment promotion.
This action aimed to encourage and promote the use of cashless
payment instruments by informing the general public about the
beneﬁts of the payment cards’ use, of remote banking service
systems, payments planned and not least the direct debit. The
need to organise such a campaign was imposed by the low level
of use of this payment instrument by the citizens of the Republic
of Moldova.
During this campaign, which has lasted six months, there were
created more spots dedicated to cashless payment instruments,
which broadcast on more national television and radio posts,
in minibuses and trolleybuses.
At the same time, there were
organised interviews, including round tables, with the participation
of banking sector experts and representatives of MasterCard and
Visa international payment systems informing about the cashless
payment instruments, beneﬁts of use and their safety, as well
the innovative solutions in this ﬁeld. It was created a website
dedicated to this topic http://www.faranumerar.md, which includes
useful information on payment instruments and materials provided
during the campaign.

During 2015, given the NBM strategic objectives, the efforts in IT
ﬁeld focused on the actions provided by two main directions: the
improvement
of
the
NBM
main
activities
through
the
implementation of a modern solution in accordance with the
objectives set out at the organization level and the consolidation
and improvement of the existing services and IT infrastructure.
More important projects have been conducted during 2015 such
as Credit Risk Register, NBM website, NBM Transform etc. in order
to improve the NBM main activities.
On another direction, NBM has taken a number of measures to
achieve the objective relating the IT infrastructure modernisation
and IT costs optimization.

virtualization capacity of IT resources in order to migrate to a
new integrated platform for network services management (mail,
electronic mail, actions printing service and ﬁles storage service

requests to develop the existing IT systems. Another moment

Cash issuance, withdrawal and processing
As from 12 October 2015, the National Bank of Moldova puts into
circulation seven newly upgraded banknotes with the face value of
MDL 1, MDL 10, MDL 20, MDL 50, MDL 100, MDL 200 and MDL 500.
The banknotes modernisation was done by supplementing them
with new security features. This procedure aimed to diminish the
risks of national currency counterfeiting.
During 2015, the National Bank of Moldova issued cash in the
total amount of MDL 7 717.0 million, of which MDL 218.0 million in
modernised banknotes, by about 29.2 percent more compared to
2014.
The banknotes issued in 2015, which in terms of value amounted
to MDL 7 710.12 million, by 29.2 percent more compared to 2014,
while in terms of quantity amounted to 112.24 million banknotes,

Given its role and duties, the internal audit is an independent
activity conducted by other organisational structures of the
institution. Thus, Internal Audit Department (IAD) examines and
evaluates independently and professionally the NBM activity
processes, providing to the governing bodies of the institution an
objective and reasonable functionality of the internal control
system and risks management system.

3.12
International Cooperation of the
Republic of Moldova
International Monetary Fund (IMF)
International Monetary Fund is a ﬁnancial institution gathering
188 member states and has as primary objectives to promote the
international monetary cooperation, to facilitate the expansion
and growth of the international trade and to ensure the foreign
exchange stability. The Republic of Moldova became member of
the International Monetary Fund on 12 August 1992 and is part of
the constituency run by the Netherlands.
As of 31 December 2015, the Republic of Moldova’s IMF quota was
SDRs 123.2 million (about USD 171 million), the equivalent of 0.05
percent of the IMF’s capital.
The 14th Review of Quotas and Reform of the Executive Board
initiated by the IMF has been completed in 2015, following the
acceptance by the USA (on 18 December 2015) of the reforms
initiated by IMF. The approval of these reforms will lead to the
duplication of the IMF current capital and to the change in
modality of creation of the IMF’s Executive Board.
Thus, all
countries, including the Republic of Moldova, which have ratiﬁed
at national level the IMF’s decisions taken in the 14th Review of
Quotas and Reform of the Executive Board shall increase their
subscription quotas at the IMF. It should be mentioned that the
Republic of Moldova has accepted in 2012 to increase its
subscription quota to the IMF by SDRs 49.3 million (from the
current quota of SDRs 123.2 million to SDRs 172.5 million), in this
regard, being adopted by the Parliament: Law on the increase of
the Republic of Moldova’s quota to the International Monetary
Fund no.109 of 11 May 2012, Law on accepting the Amendment
of the IMF Articles of Agreement on the Reform of the Executive
Board no.118 of 24 May 2012.
During 2015, the NBM cooperation with the IMF was conducted
in the context of the consultation mission under Article IV of the
IMF’s Statutes45.
45Regular consultations provided for in Article IV of the IMF Statute is a supervisory
exercise which is mandatory for all member states, following their accession to the
IMF.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
93
This mission also included the aspects relating to the post-program
monitoring46 and the discussion on policies, which may form the
basis for a new programme supported by the IMF.
In this context, a team of IMF experts conducted, during the
period of 22 September - 6 October 2015, a working visit in
Chis, in˘au. The IMF experts with the representative authorities of
the Republic of Moldova have discussed during the meetings the
priorities of the reforms for 2016, in order to enhance the banking
system, to ensure the macroeconomic activity and to strengthen
the regulation, supervision and management framework of the
Republic of Moldova’s crises.
The last IMF assistance program for the Republic of Moldova has
been
completed
in
April
2013
and
included
two
funding
mechanisms: in concessional terms (Extended Crediting Facility
(ECF)) and in non-concessional terms (Extended Fund Facility
(EFF)), respectively.
In this context, the Republic of Moldova
received ﬁnancial assistance in the amount of SDRs 320.0 million,
of which SDRs 225.0 million were received by the National Bank of
Moldova and, respectively, SDRs 95.0 million - by the Ministry of
Finance (Table A.27).
In 2015, total payments related to the servicing of loans granted
by the IMF to the National Bank of Moldova constituted SDRs 26.61
million (equivalent to USD 37.30 million), of which the payments
for the principal amount of the loan constituted SDRs 25.10 million
(equivalent to USD 35.18 million) and payments for interest rates
and commissions on loans - SDRs 1.51 million (equivalent to USD
2.12 million).
European Union (EU)
During 2015, the relations between the Republic of Moldova and
EU have been conducted mainly in the context of fulﬁlling the
commitments set out in the Association Agreement between the
Republic of Moldova and European Union (signed in Brussels,
Belgium on 27 June 2014 and applied on a provisional basis as
from 1 September 2014) and the commitments arising from other
documents and Moldova - EU agreements.
In order to meet the commitments provided in the Association
Agreement with the European Union, the Government of the
Republic of Moldova approved (in October 2014) the National
Action Plan for the implementation of the Moldova-EU Association
Agreement in 2014-2016 (NAP) - a broad document, which
contributes to the political association and economic integration
of the Republic of Moldova with the European Union. NAP is the
basic instrument to monitor during three years the European
integration process at the national level. The NBM participated in
the NAP elaboration and is responsible to achieve some actions
included in categories III, IV and V of this plan and shall report on
progress made in this regard to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and
European Integration and Ministry of Economy.
Thus,
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova
contributes
to
the
implementation of this Plan through a series of targeted actions,
46Post-program monitoring is a process that involves all member states after the
expiry of IMF-supported programs and aims to ensure the viability of the country’s
economic framework and early warning about the state’s ability to repay its debt
to the IMF.


94
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
such as alignment of the legislation on the central bank with the
best EU practices, NBM capacity building in banking regulation
and supervision, development and strengthening governance
related requirements and bank risk management, transposition
and
implementation
of
EU
legislation
in
the
ﬁeld
of
ﬁnancial-banking services etc.
During 2015, the NBM continued to participate in the coordination
process of the European integration activities at the national level.
Thus, the Governor and First Deputy Governor of the NBM have
participated in the meetings of the Governmental Commission for
European Integration of the Republic of Moldova,
which is
responsible
to
ensure
the
coordination
for
fulﬁlling
the
commitments
arising
from
Moldova-EU
documents
and
agreements. Within the Commission meetings, it was discussed,
among other issues, the progress in NAP achievement, the NAP
modiﬁcations and updates, the priorities for 2015, the DCFTA
implementation, the preparation for the Moldova-EU Association
Council47, which was carried out in Brussels on 16 March 2015.
In the context of collaboration between the Republic of Moldova
and
European
Union
and
in
order
to
ensure
an
efﬁcient
communication
on
the
progress
made
for
fulﬁlling
the
commitments undertaken in Association Agreement, the NBM
representatives
have
participated
during
2015
within
the
international cooperation formats, such as:
The Association
Committee brought together into trade conﬁguration (4-6 March
and 22 October), cluster I of the Subcommittee for economic and
sectoral cooperation (23-24 July), ﬁrst meeting of the Moldova-EU
Association Committee (19 October).
In this regard, the NBM
submitted to national authorities (the Ministry of Foreign Affairs
and European Integration, the Ministry of Economy, the Ministry of
Finance, the State Chancellery) information on macroeconomic
situation, the developments in ﬁnancial-banking ﬁeld and progress
in harmonization of national legislation with the EU acquit.
At the same time, the Moldova-EU economic dialogue was also
carried out through the cooperation of the Republic of Moldova
authorities, including the NBM with the experts contracted by the
Directorate-General for Economic and Financial Affairs (DG ECFIN)
of the European Commission. The purposes of the team delegated
by DG ECFIN was to carry out an operational assessment of the
Republic of Moldova authorities, in the context of macroeconomic
assistance, which is to be offered to the Republic of Moldova. Thus,
during the fourth quarter of 2015, the NBM submitted, at the DG
ECFIN experts’ request, information on the recent developments
in the banking system of the Republic of Moldova.
During 2015, the dialogue of the Republic of Moldova with the
EU continued to strengthen in terms of acquiring the experience
and best practices of the EU central banks in various ﬁelds falling
within the NBM area of competence. In this context, the NBM
representatives received assistance, in various forms, through the
TAIEX instrument (workshop - 1, study visits - 3). At the same
time, in the bilateral cooperation context, the BNM representatives
conducted one working visit at the National Bank of Romania, with
the purpose of acquiring the experience in the cash management
ﬁeld.
47The Association Council is a meeting at senior ofﬁcial level, set out in accordance
with the provisions of the Association Agreement, which is aimed at supervising
and monitoring the Association Agreement application.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
95
As from 1 July 2015, the NBM received assistance from the
European
Union
through
Twinning
instrument,
in
order
to
strengthen the central bank capacity in the banking supervision
and
regulation
ﬁeld
in
the
context
of
implementing
the
requirements of Basel II/III.
Group of Banking Supervisors from Central and Eastern
Europe (BSCEE)
In order to promote and maintain a close cooperation between
the BSCEE members in the banking supervision ﬁeld, the NBM
representatives have participated in the regional forum hosted
by the Czech National Bank in Prague on 5-7 May 2015.
The
event aimed at sharing experiences and issues related to the
Basel III implementation between the banking supervisors from
Central and Eastern Europe. The main discussions focused on the
ﬁnancial stability reforms, Basel III implementation, strengthening
the liquidity principle and the normative framework related to
credit, operational and market risks.
During 2015, in the context of cooperation relations with BSCEE,
the NBM representatives have also participated in the 28-th Annual
conference, with the theme: "Improving the supervision of the
ﬁnancial sector and the current objectives in regulation", which was
carried out in Bratislava, Slovakia on 24-26 June 2015. The subjects
discussed in the conference were related to the strengthening of
the single supervisory mechanism and the revision of the Basel
III standards in order to determine the capital requirements, the
potential impact of the ﬁnancial crimes in the virtual environment
on the banks’ activity etc.
The National Bank of Moldova continued working with the members
of BSCEE group during the reporting year through the exchange
of information and experience in the banking supervision and
regulation ﬁeld. Thus, following the request of the National Bank
of Serbia, the NBM submitted an information about the national
normative framework and practices relating to the cession of non-
performing loans.
External
technical
assistance
and
cooperation
with
international
institutions
and
central
banks
of
other
states
Strengthening the capacity of regulation and supervision of a
central bank has a signiﬁcant role in reﬂecting a solid and
competitive sector.
In this regard, National Bank of Moldova
together with National Bank of Romania and Netherlands Central
Bank have launched on 30 June 2015 the project Twinning for
strengthening
the
NBM’s
capacity
in
the
ﬁeld
of
banking
regulation and supervision.
The project was ﬁnanced by the
European Union and will be carried out during a period of 2 years
with the support of these two partner banks.
In this context, a series of activities have been undertaken during
the launching period of the Twinning project till the end of 2015,
to implement the Basel III Capital Agreement, respectively, some
commitments derived from the Association Agreement signed with
the EU through which the Republic of Moldova has committed to
harmonise its legislation with the EU provisions. In particular, these
activities aimed at transposing into the domestic legislation of the
Directive 2013/36/EU and Regulation 575/2013. Thus, among the
progresses made in this respect, the followings may be mentioned:


96
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
• the national legislation framework has been assessed in
the context of its compliance with the provisions of the EU
legislation regarding the Basel III. There were identiﬁed the
gaps and, respectively, the areas which need to be improved
(Legal gap analysis);
• the questionnaire drawing up process has been initiated to
assess the impact of the transition to the requirements of
Basel III and it is remitted to banks for its completion in the
ﬁrst quarter of 2016;
• subjects of major importance have been discussed, which
are to be negotiated and a common decision of the NBM
and NCFM is to be taken in respect to them, to develop the
Strategy for implementing the framework of Basel III at the
ﬁnancial system level;
• the drawing up process of the NBM Strategy for the
implementation of Basel III standards through the European
legislative framework (CRD IV);
• there were assessed the needs of staff and specialized
trainings in the regulation and supervision ﬁeld to comply
with the new requirements.
The activities carried out within the Twinning Project aimed at
enhancing
signiﬁcantly
the
banking
regulation
and
the
development of supervision instruments available to ensure an
adequate management of risks in banks, comparable to the best
practices of the European Union in this regard.
It is important to establish a relevant interaction procedure
between the banking supervisory authorities in order to promote
the ﬁnancial stability worldwide and to ensure an efﬁcient
cooperation between these authorities. Thus, in November 2015,
the National Bank of Moldova and the banking supervisory body of
Latvia (Financial and Capital Market Commission of Latvia)
concluded the Memorandum of understanding on the exchange of
information in the banking supervision and money laundering
ﬁeld.
The National Bank of Moldova still orients its efforts toward
achieving the goal of maintaining ﬁnancial stability of the banking
system in the Republic of Moldova. In this context, the National
Bank
of
Moldova
has
received
during
2015
the
technical
assistance from the US Treasury on ensuring the continuity of the
activities related to banking supervision, the ways to develop
plans
for
resolution
of
systemically
important
banks
and
international practices relating to contingency plans.
In 2015, the National Bank of Moldova has beneﬁted from advice
on tax subjects within the process of monetary policy formulation
to improve the forecast capacity and to ensure the price stability.
The technical assistance on these subjects was offered by the
European Bank for Reconstruction and Development.
One of the basic duties of the NBM is to hold and manage the
foreign exchange reserves of the State. During 2015, NBM has
received technical assistance from the World Bank (in accordance
with the Investment Consulting and Management Agreement
signed
on
8
December
2015),
in
order
to
optimise
the
management process of the credit risk in the foreign exchange


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
97
management activity. The assistance mission was focused on the
review of management process of the credit and counterparty
risk; setting the limits of credit risks; familiarisation with the World
Bank practices in credit risk management; new tendencies in
credit risk exposure for central banks when the interest rates are
very low or even negative; effects of the regulatory modiﬁcations
on international ﬁnancial market over the counterparty credit risk.
At the same time, given the commitments assumed by the Republic
of Moldova in the context of the European integration process, the
National Bank of Moldova has received support in more areas
from the European Commission through the TAIEX assistance
instrument.
Thus, in the context of the NBM actions regarding the creation of
the single Central Securities Depositary (CSD) to increase the
safety and efﬁciency of the depository and settlement system for
the securities of the Republic of Moldova, the NBM has received
technical assistance through the TAIEX instrument during the
period 27-28 January 2015, in the form of a study visit at the
Central Bank of Hungary on the topic "Creation of the single
Central Securities Depository". The study visit purpose was the
familiarisation with the regulation, operating and supervision
framework of the single Central Securities Depository of Hungary
(Keller) to identify the CSD business model relevant to the
situation of the Republic of Moldova.
The cashless payments promotion and cash in circulation decrease
is one the NBM strategic objective. In this regard, NBM received
technical assistance through the TAIEX instrument on 18-19 March
2015, in the form of a study visit at the Central Bank of Sweden on
the topic "Cashless payments promotion". The study visit purpose
was the familiarisation with the Sweden practices on the regulation
framework relating to the cashless payments promotion, including
the ﬁscal incentives for the use of cashless payment instruments.
The National Bank of Moldova, as issuer and manager of the
banknotes on the territory of the Republic of Moldova, put into
circulation banknotes and coins through the banking system. In
order to ensure the national currency circulation stability, the NBM
has as objective to promote of the policy that ensure the economy
in national currency under the principles of continuity and
credibility in line with the real needs of the monetary circulation
and the maintenance of their quality. In this context, the NBM has
beneﬁted during 2015 of technical assistance with the topic “Cash
Management” from the National Bank of Hungary through the
TAIEX instrument and obtained of technical assistance offered
bilaterally from central banks of Georgia and Romania.
The
technical assistance consisted of some study visits of the NBM
representatives at the aforementioned central banks. The purpose
of this visit consisted of taking the experience of other central
banks in order to manage, hold and process the cash and to
ensure the treasury security in a central bank. At the same time,
there have been discussed a series of subjects related to the
optimization
of
cash
management
processes;
the
highest
standards of a treasury security; transparency and fully control
due to the highest monitoring of cash circulation, measures in the
ﬁeld of national currency falsiﬁcation and counterfeiting etc.
Following these study visits, the NBM will initiate more projects on
cash management optimization.


98
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
The NBM representatives have beneﬁted on 29-30 September 2015
of a training provided by the representatives of the National Bank
of Hungary through the TAIEX instrument in the ﬁeld of supervision,
recovery and resolution of the credit institutions and investment
companies. This training aimed at exchanging experience on the
transposition and implementations issues of the EU legislation
with regards to the banking supervision authorities and banking
resolution.
An efﬁcient performance management plays an important role in
creating an incentive and attractive work environment, where
the employees perform at maximum capacity to achieve the
strategic objectives of the institution. In this regard, it should
be mentioned that the representatives of the NBM carried out
during 2015 a study visit at the Central Bank of the Netherlands
with the thematic ”Performance management for the NBM”. This
assistance focused on the aspects related to implementation of a
performance assessment system of the National Bank of Moldova
employees.
In order to enhance the stability and conﬁdence in ﬁnancial sector
and to strengthen the economic development as a whole, it is
important to support the consumers’ ﬁnancial responsibility and to
promote ﬁnancial education among the population. The National
Bank of Moldova is oriented to participate in the ﬁnancial education
promotion process, encouraging other relevant institutions in this
regard. In accordance with the provisions of the Roadmap matrix
on competitiveness improvement (approved by the Government
Decision no.4 of 14 January 2014), the NBM together with other
relevant authorities committed to draw up the National Strategy
for Financial Education. It should be mentioned that the NBM plays
the leader role in the strategy drawing up process.
Thus, in August 2015, the National Bank of Moldova jointly with
Development Facility of the European Fund for South-East Europe
(EFSE DF) and regional Microﬁnance Centre (MFC) of Poland have
signed a consultancy contract, which provides the support of the
MFC experts’ team to the NBM in the process of drawing up the
National Strategy for Financial Education.
The technical assistance project has been initiated in September
2015 and during the second semester of 2015 the MFC experts
carried out two study visits at Chis, in˘au, where they participated in
a series of meetings with the representatives of the ministries,
authorities and institutions of the Republic of Moldova, the activity
of which is important for the subject of ﬁnancial education
promotion.
At the same time, two workshops have been conducted within
the NBM, where the representatives of relevant institutions of the
Republic of Moldova have participated. First workshop, conducted
on 25 September 2015 aimed at strengthening and generalizing
the priorities, preferences and visions of the institutions with
regards to the drawing up process of the National Strategy for
Financial Education.
The second workshop, conducted on 15
December 2015 aimed at submitting by the MFC experts the
assessment results on the ﬁnancial education level of our country
population and the concept of the National Strategy for Financial
Education.

In the context of ﬁnancial education promotion, the National Bank
of Moldova has beneﬁted of technical and ﬁnancial assistance
from the European Fund for South East Europe (EFSE) to organize
seminars of ﬁnancial education for pupils and training-professors
from various districts of the Republic of Moldova, celebrating on

and consequently, an efﬁcient contribution to ﬁnancial inclusion.
In addition, the European Fund for South East Europe has also

Activity
of
the
Council
of
