National Bank of Moldova in 2015
There were held 44 meetings of the Council of Administration of
the National Bank of Moldova during 2015, where 222 decisions
have been examined and approved.
It should be mentioned that the Council of Administration has been
substituted by the Executive Board and Supervisory Board since 21
August 2015, as a result of the amendment of Law on the National
Bank of Moldova no.548-XIII of 21 July 1995. Thus, there were
held 5 meetings of the Supervisory Board during 2015, where 7
decisions have been examined and approved and 31 meetings of
the Executive Board, where 173 decisions have been examined
and approved.
Decisions aimed at the following areas:
• Monetary and Foreign Exchange Policy of the NBM;
• Payments System Oversight Policy of the Republic of Moldova;
• Accounting Policy of the National Bank of Moldova;
• Banking Regulation and Supervision;
• Bookkeeping;
• Balance of Payments.
During 2015, 30 decisions of the Council of Administration, 2
decisions of the Supervisory Board and 20 decisions of the
Executive Board (according to Table A.34 and Table A.35 from
annex) have been submitted for publication in the Ofﬁcial Monitor
of the Republic of Moldova.


100
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
3.14
Legislative activity
Foreign exchange regulation
In 2015, the National Bank of Moldova adopted and/or amended
normative acts related to foreign exchange regulation, which are
focused on aspects of calculation of foreign exchange open position
of banks, authorisation, opening, management and closing of
residents’ accounts aboard, and banks’ reporting to the NBM on
foreign exchange operations.
In order to avoid situations when banks regulate open foreign
exchange position by entering into off-balance sheet foreign
exchange transactions related to the purchase / sale of foreign
currency against MDL (e.g., forward transactions), which do not
end with settlements between participants, the Regulation on
banks
open
foreign
exchange
position
was
amended
and
supplemented in 2015.
The main amendments refer to the following aspects:
• for the off-balance sheet transactions of purchase/sale of
foreign currency against MDL, there were established criteria
whose observance allows to include these transactions in the
calculation of foreign currency position;
• for the calculation of the open foreign currency position of
banks, the balances of foreign currency assets and liabilities
will be reduced by the balances of the off-balance sheet
accounts of purchase/sale of foreign currency against MDL,
which
do
not
correspond
to
these
criteria,
and
the
off-balance sheet transactions of purchase/sale of foreign
currency against MDL and against foreign currency that did
not end with settlements,
the latest comprising those
transactions
whose
maturity
was
extended.
The
above-mentioned transactions excluded from the calculation
of open foreign exchange position shall be reﬂected in a
separate table in the report in order to monitor bank’s
activity and the risks to which it is exposed;
• banks are obliged to recalculate the open foreign exchange
position and to submit to the NBM corrected reports, where for
certain days when calculating open foreign exchange position
there were included the balances of the off-balance sheet
accounts subject to exclusion as indicated above;
• when, as a result of the recalculation of the open foreign
exchange position, the limit of the open foreign exchange
position is exceeded at the end of the respective days, the
licensed bank is obliged to submit to the NBM, at the request,
any
document
related
to
the
respective
transactions/operations;
• in order to avoid manipulation by banks in calculating open
foreign
exchange
position
in
the
context
of
the
new
requirements, there were set out the sanctions / remedial
measures that may be applied by the NBM for violating the
provisions of the Regulation by exceeding the open foreign
exchange position, due to its recalculation;


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
101
• in connection with the new above-mentioned requirements,
the procedure for compiling the report on open foreign
exchange position was adjusted.
These amendments were aimed to discourage the use by banks
of transactions of foreign currency purchase / sale maintained in
the off-balance sheet accounts for regulating artiﬁcial open foreign
currency position, which should help to minimize the involvement
of banks in risky transactions.
Additionally, to exclude distortion of open foreign exchange
position of the bank through erroneous evaluation of foreign
exchange assets / liabilities, there were included in the report on
open foreign exchange position the contra-accounts for the
adjustment of the value at amortised cost and the allowances for
impairment losses on foreign exchange assets and liabilities /
linked to foreign currency, regardless of the currency (national or
foreign currency) in which the records are kept in the respective
contra-accounts for the adjustments/allowances.
During the year, the regulatory framework that governs aspects
related to accounts opened by residents in foreign ﬁnancial
institutions has been updated. The approval of the Regulation on
resident accounts abroad and the abrogation of the previous
instruction was required by the need to improve the regulatory
framework, given the practical aspects related to authorisation,
opening, management and closing of accounts and the Law on
foreign exchange regulation and related legislation.
This
Regulation addresses issues related to:
• process of authorisation of opening of accounts abroad by
the National Bank of Moldova;
• peculiarities related to the opening, operating and closing of
accounts abroad, whose opening requires authorisation from
the National Bank of Moldova;
• reporting to the National Bank of Moldova on accounts opened
abroad based on the NBM authorisation;
• peculiarities related to the application of sanctions against
holders of authorisations issued by the NBM;
• some peculiarities related to accounts abroad, whose opening
abroad does not require authorisation from the National Bank
of Moldova.
At the same time, the regulatory framework that regulates the
reporting process of the banks to the NBM has been further
optimised. Thus, in the context of transition to the new reporting
system by licensed banks to the NBM (SIRBNM) and to improve
statistical data, the reports related to the export and import from /
to the Republic of Moldova of cash by banks have been amended.
The new report is divided into three separate tables, covering the
entire spectrum of transactions in question provided for by the
Law no.62-XVI of 21 March 2008 on foreign exchange regulation.
The report on resident accounts opened abroad under the NBM
authorisation was supplemented with additional information in the
context of the balance of payments compilation.


102
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
Prudential regulation
In order to increase safety in banks’ activity, based on the powers
and rights conferred to the National Bank by the Law on the
National Bank of Moldova, there have been taken in 2015 a number
of actions related to prudential regulation aimed at strengthening
the domestic banking sector by improving banking regulatory
framework.
The Instruction on the preparation and submission of reports by
banks
for
prudential
purposes
has
been
amended
and
supplemented with regard to the reporting framework for
continuous monitoring of banks and improving the methods of
banking supervision.
Thus, in order to protect the interests of depositors and to avoid
excessive risks in the banking sector, it has been included a set of
reports related to aggregate information on deposits of individuals
by their size, location and maturity.
In order to monitor and prevent conﬂicts of interest, limit the risks
of concentration of bank exposures to afﬁliates and to protect the
interests of bank customers, the reporting framework has been
improved by requiring more detailed information on transactions
with afﬁliated persons and on the bank’s Board meetings.
The calculation of risk weighted capital adequacy has been also
amended by including in the category of risk 50% only the credits
fully secured by residential mortgage, which is the property of the
debtor individual.
It should be mentioned that banking activity represents, in
essence, a process of continuous improvement, which requires
administrators of banks to have certain skills. Thus, the Regulation
on requirements to bank’s administrators has been amended and
completed to ensure continuous improvement of the mechanism
of conﬁrming persons for the position of bank administrators.
To develop a special assessment framework of persons entrusted
by law or statute to assume obligations, alone or with others
in the name and on behalf of the bank, other than member of
Board, executive body, Audit Committee, chief accountant, branch
administrator, new assessment criteria have been set out, taking
into account the speciﬁc nature of the obligations of the respective
persons.
To promote persons in banks that correspond to the ﬁt and proper
criteria, new requirements have been established to conﬁrm their
awareness and obligations to comply with the provisions of
relevant laws and normative acts in the exercise of the function of
administrator. There have also been speciﬁed the requirements
for theoretical practical experience of candidates for the position
of administrator, namely that the majority of members of the
bank’s Board to be persons with experience as administrator in
the banking and non-banking ﬁnancial market.
To strengthen the capacity of banks for a safe ﬁnancial activity, the
Regulation on risk-weighted capital adequacy was supplemented


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
103
with provisions on sanctions applicable to infringements of the
conditions prescribed in the permission of the National Bank and on
the adoption of a decision of the general meeting of shareholders
on capital distribution without NBM permission.
These provisions are aimed at strengthening the regulatory
framework related to distribution of capital by banks. Given the
importance of maintaining the ﬁnancial soundness of banks on
the one hand and ensuring the predictability and transparency of
the assessment of applications for the distribution of capital, on
the other hand, there have been detailed the regulations in this
ﬁeld by establishing dynamic criteria that will enable a complex
analysis of the bank’s ability to meet the conditions stipulated by
the Law on ﬁnancial institutions no.550-XIII of 21 July 1995.
At the same time, in order to ensure access of shareholders to
the results of the assessment by the National Bank of bank’s
request for permission to distribute capital, it has been established
the obligation to include this permission in the agenda of the
meeting of the management body which will examine the question
of distribution of capital.
Starting with the purpose of the Regulation on large exposures,
namely minimizing losses that the bank may incur as a result of
the concentration of exposures and given the persistence of
certain transactions bearing increased credit risk, a mechanism
for minimizing risks has been established, which does not restrict
ﬁnancial market and its proper functioning.
Thus,
when
determining the amount of net exposures, only irrevocable
pledged deposits are included in the calculation as security, which
holders
are
directly
the
domestic
debtors
or
international
organizations, multilateral development banks and / or banks that
have a rating not lower than BBB-/Baa3 assigned by at least one
of the agencies Standard & Poor’s, Moody’s and Fitch-IBCA,
simultaneously being residents of countries with such ratings.
In order to optimize the process of classiﬁcation and calculation
of the amount of allowances for impairment losses on assets and
contingent liabilities of the bank subject to credit risk, it was
established in the Regulation on contingent assets and liabilities
classiﬁcation the monthly frequency classiﬁcation and calculation
of allowances for impairment losses on assets and contingent
liabilities of the bank subject to credit risk. As for the prolonged
and renegotiated credits / contingent liabilities, the period of
monitoring of regular payment of principal amount and related
interest rates, and the ﬁnancial situation of the debtor, has been
extended from 3 to 6 months.
There were also included new principles of classiﬁcation of
contingent assets / liabilities, which are determined based on the
counterparty’s rating assigned by one of the international rating
agencies
Standard
&
Poor’s,
Moody’s
and
Fitch-IBCA.
Simultaneously, there have been included certain assessment
requirements to the person that can provide a personal guarantee
(suretyship) in favour of bank’s debtor.
In order to minimize the risks involved in lending activity of the
bank,
there
have
been
provided
for
assets
classiﬁcation
peculiarities, depending on the size of debtor’s debt prevalence to
its own funds. This requirement shall not apply to credits and
contingent liabilities whose cumulative value is less than MDL 1


104
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
million and those secured by at least 50% of total exposure to the
debtor with sufﬁcient collateral. These provisions are aimed at
minimizing the share of credits with an increased level of
indebtedness that can become non-performing.
Considering developments in recent years in the ﬁnancial market,
namely the reduction of banks in the market and taking into
account the aim to create conditions to exclude impediments
to expansion of ﬁnancial activity of banks, there have been made
changes to the Regulation on mergers and absorptions of banks,
as a result of which the maximum limit of dominant positions on
the banking market related to the bank’s total assets compared to
total assets of the entire banking sector was amended from 30%
to 35% , as well as that of individuals’ deposits of banks to total
deposits received individuals in the entire banking sector.
Starting from the fact that bank liquidity is a management position
of bank assets and liabilities with different maturities, it is difﬁcult
for banks to be in a position of perfect match, moreover banking
operations have uncertain maturities and are of a great variety.
Match or mismatch between maturities of assets and liabilities is
fundamental to the management of banking activity. Thus, new
requirements were established to minimize the risk of liquidity
caused by the mismatch of assets and liabilities of banks. In this
context, the Regulation on bank’s liquidity was supplemented with
provisions related to the Principle III of liquidity, which provides
for the requirement to liquidity based on the following maturity
bands: up to 1 month, 1-3 months, 3-6 months, 6-12 months and
over 12 months. The minimum limit of this indicator is 1 and is
calculated as the ratio of actual liquidity and required liquidity by
each maturity band. Actual liquidity is determined by summing,
on each maturity band, the assets and credit contingent liabilities
(those based on which are expected cash inﬂows).
Required
liquidity is determined by summing, on each maturity band, the
liabilities and debit contingent liabilities (those based on which are
expected cash outﬂows). In case of a surplus of liquidity on any of
the maturity bands, except for the last band, it will be added to
the actual liquidity for the next maturity band.
Payment systems regulation
According to the Law on payment services and electronic money
no. 114 of 18 May 2012, the National Bank of Moldova (NBM)
licenses, regulates and supervises the activity of payment service
providers and electronic money issuers.
In accordance with this Law, during 2015, it was issued a license
for the activity of issuing electronic money and were reissued three
licenses for the activity of providing payment services and issuing
electronic money.
By the Decision of the Council of Administration no.56 of 5 March
2015,
the Regulation on credit transfer was amended and
supplemented with regards to the use of the IBAN (International
Bank Account Number) for national transfers,
in order to
implement
the
Concept
of
national
transfers
optimisation,
approved by the Decision of the Council of Administration of the
NBM no.138 of 24 July 2014. The need to implement the IBAN
code for payments in national currency in the Republic of Moldova


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
105
was determined by both the experience of other states in the
implementation of IBAN code and the advantages of its use:
minimisation of erroneous data (by standardizing the IBAN
structure) and processing time (by applying the STP mechanism),
as well as reduction of transfer costs (by minimizing the manual
intervention and introducing automated veriﬁcation).
New amendments and additions to the Regulation on the activity
of non-bank electronic money issuers and non-bank payment
service providers have been approved by the Decision of the
Council of Administration no.105 of 9 April 2015, which are aimed
at detailing the requirements for the branches and agencies of
payment services providers / postal service providers / electronic
money issuers and setting requirements for frequency, form and
content of reports on regulatory capital.
Also, by the Decision no.217 of 20 August 2015 of the Council of
Administration, there were approved amendments and additions to
the Regulation on payment cards, aimed to ensure a higher level
of security and safety in operation and availability of settlements
processes related to operations in national currency made with
payment cards in the Republic of Moldova. These amendments
were derived from the need to eliminate risks and increase trust
of participants in these processes.


106
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
3.15
National
Bank
of
Moldova
employees
and
professional
development
According to the Strategic Plan of the National Bank of Moldova, the
Human Resources Department is responsible for the achievement
of two of the ten strategic objectives: optimisation of human
resources management and corporate culture development.
In 2015, this optimisation took place through several staff
structural reorganizations, without employees losing their jobs. As
at 31 December 2015, the NBM payroll was completed at the level
of 98.11 percent (478 permanent employees).
In 2015, the NBM employed 49 persons, other 54 persons resigned,
16 persons were promoted and 127 persons were transferred to
similar positions in other subdivisions.
A number of 18 students completed their practical internship at
the NBM, 4 out of which were employed with the NBM at the end
of their practical internship. In 2015, there have been organised 3
editions of induction tour programmes, which aimed to facilitate
the integration of new employees in the institution.
The average age of a NBM employee is 40.5 years. Nearly two
thirds are women (62.47 percent) and nearly one-third (37.53
percent) are men.
At the level of heads of subdivisions, the situation is currently 25/70
percent. Almost 64.81 percent (304 employees) have children and
59.06 percent (277 employees) are part of syndicates.
Average year of service of employees is 10 years and 1 months.
The most majority of employees completed higher education -
79.96 percent, with the exception of technical staff.
There are 8 employees who hold a PhD degree in economics or
technical sciences (1.71 percent), 95 employees have Master’s
Degree (20.26 percent), and other 280 employees have higher
education (59.70 percent). During 2015, a number of 6 employees
completed their postgraduate studies.
Over the year, about 151 employees participated in 127 trainings
abroad and 32 trainings organized in the Republic of Moldova,
ACAP certiﬁcation courses, English language courses, which
contribute to their personal and professional growth and greater
work motivation. Employees who participated in training courses
abroad
beneﬁted
from
sponsorship
from
international
organizations and the NBM.
In 2015, several events that contributed to the strengthening of
the corporate culture were organized, such as the organisation of
volleyball competitions, various tourist excursions.
The sixth edition of the survey on employees’ satisfaction was
organised in 2015, which evaluated their rate of satisfaction on
various aspects of work, level of involvement, development, etc.
During the reporting period, the performance management system
has been implemented within the NBM.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
107
3.16
Analysis of the ﬁnancial situation
of the National Bank of Moldova
General considerations
In accordance with the Law on the National Bank of Moldova
no.548-XIII of 21 July 1995 (republished in the Ofﬁcial Monitor of
the Republic of Moldova, 2015, no.
297-300, Art.554), the
fundamental objective of the National Bank of Moldova is to
achieve and maintain price stability.
The basic functions of the NBM are provided in the Law on the
National Bank of Moldova. In accordance with the legal provisions,
the NBM operations are performed in order to promote and support
the monetary and foreign exchange policy in the State, to ensure
stability of the banking system, national currency issuance, foreign
exchange reserves management etc. Accordingly, maximizing the
proﬁt from the activity of the NBM is not a purpose itself and cannot
serve as an impediment to achieve its fundamental objective and
functions.
Proﬁt available for distribution
The ﬁnancial results of the NBM are directly dependent on the need
to conduct monetary and foreign exchange operations in order to
achieve the fundamental objective and fulﬁl the basic tasks of the
NBM. Evolution of proﬁt available for distribution during 2013-2015
is shown in chart 3.80.
At the end of 2015, National Bank recorded a proﬁt available
for distribution of MDL 1,960.89 million (in 2014: MDL 1,778.73
Chart 3.80: Evolution of proﬁt available for distribution
(MDL, million)
250.08
1,778.73
1,960.89
Source: NBM
million).
Article 20 of the Law on the National Bank of Moldova no.548-XIII
of 21 July 1995 was amended by the Law on amending and
supplementing certain normative acts no.147 of 30 July 2015,
which stipulates the following: "At the end of the ﬁnancial year,
the proﬁt available for distribution will be allocated for the
statutory capital increase within the limits provided for in Article
19 paragraph (3)".
Thus, the limit of statutory capital set out in Article 19 paragraph
(3) of the Law on the National Bank of Moldova is 10% of total
monetary liabilities of the National Bank.
As at 31 December 2015, the level of statutory capital in total
monetary obligations was about 8.88% (as at 31 December 2014:
2.67%) and was structured as follows:1/3 - authorised capital (MDL
902,970 thousand) and 2/3 - general reserve fund (MDL 1,805,940
thousand).
In these circumstances, the proﬁt available for distribution for 2015
was fully allocated at the end of the ﬁnancial year to increase
the statutory capital, within the conditions and limits provided


108
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
for in Article 19 paragraph (3) of the Law on the National Bank
of Moldova.
Thus, the proﬁt available for distribution was not
allocated to State budget as the level of statutory capital failed to
reach to limit of 10% as required by law.
The main factors that had a signiﬁcant impact on the proﬁt
available for distribution are as follows:
• The increase of income from foreign exchange reserves
management
with
about
MDL
740
million,
which
is
characterised by the following developments:
– increase of income from operations in foreign currency
by about MDL 754 million, due to the depreciation of the
national currency against main foreign currencies, the
income being mainly obtained as a result of currency
sales;
– decrease of income from the revaluation of securities in
foreign currency by about MDL 12 million, due to positive
revaluation of securities in foreign currency at maturity
or at the date of sale;
– decrease of income from interest on securities and
investments in foreign currency with about MDL 2
million, due to the decrease of portfolio of securities held
to maturity.
• The increase of income from the amortisation of the discount
on SS held in the NBM portfolio by about MDL 237 million
compared to 2014, due to higher interest rates on SS held in
the NBM portfolio compared to 2014.
• Increase of income from interest on loans granted and repo
operations approximately by MDL 63 million, generated by
the following developments:
– increase of income from interest rates on short-term
credits granted to banks by about MDL 8 million;
– increase of income from interest on repo operations by
approximately MDL 58 million, due to a higher volume of
repo operations and with greater interest rates;
– decrease of income from interest rates on overnight
credits by about MDL 3 million, as a result of reduced use
of overnight credit facility by banks.
• The increase of expenses on interest rates on required
reserves of banks by about MDL 538 million compared to
2014, as a result of higher required reserves ratio in MDL and
higher rate applied on remuneration of required reserves in
MDL.
• The increase of expenses on amortisation of the discount on
NBM Certiﬁcates by about MDL 63 million compared to 2014,
as a result of higher interest rates on NBM Certiﬁcates.
• The increase of expenses on interest rates on overnight
deposits by about MDL 62 million compared to the same
period of 2014, inﬂuenced by both the increase in interest
rates on monetary policy instruments of the NBM and the
increase
of
the
average
daily
balance
of
overnight
placements.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
109
• The increase of costs for national currency by about MDL
116 million over the same period of 2014, due to higher
production volumes.
• The increase of expenses from the differences on revaluation
of securities in foreign currency by about MDL 25 million
compared to 2014, determined by the fact that a larger
number of securities in foreign currency reached their
maturity, positively revaluated in the previous years, both
the securities managed by the NBM and securities managed
by the World Bank under the Agreement for investment
management and consulting.
• The increase of expenses from interest rates on demand
deposits from the Ministry of Finance by about MDL 6 million,
as a result of the increase in 2015 of the balance of demand
deposits of the Ministry of Finance and of the interest rates
as compared to the previous year.
The proﬁt available for distribution is presented in table A.28 by
activities of the National Bank of Moldova conducted during 2015.
Impact of monetary policy implementation and activities
with regard to the relations with the Government on the
ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank of Moldova
National Bank of Moldova continued to use liquidity absorbing
instruments throughout 2015. During the reporting period, the
average daily balance of NBM Certiﬁcates was MDL 783.36 million
(in 2014: MDL 1,623.91 million). At the same time, the costs of
sterilization operations through the issuance of NBM Certiﬁcates
increased, accounting for about MDL 120 million in 2015 compared
to MDL 57 million in 2014. The increase of the expenses related
to the amortisation of the discount on the NBM Certiﬁcates was
determined by higher interest rates on NBM Certiﬁcates. Thus, the
average interest rate on NBM Certiﬁcates was 15.45 percent in
2015 (in 2014: 3.51 percent). In 2015, the NBM carried also out
repo operations to inject liquidity in the interbank money market.
The average daily balance of repo operations accounted to MDL
538.73 million. Thus, the average interest rate on repo operations
was 14.89 percent in 2015 (in 2014: 4.12 percent).
The monetary policy instruments applied by the National Bank in
2015 and the activities with regard to the relations with the
Government
generated
a
net
loss
of
MDL
368.62
million,
representing an increase of 37,171.99 percent compared to 2014
(Table A.29).
Impact of state foreign exchange reserves management
operations on the ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank
of Moldova
Foreign exchange reserves of the State held by the NBM decreased
by 18.54 percent as at 31 December 2015 or by USD 399.83 million
compared to 31 December 2014 (from USD 2,156.63 million to
USD 1,756.81 million), invested mainly in instruments in USD, EUR


110
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
and GBP. The average annual volume of international reserves
decreased by 34.47 percent in 2015 or by about USD 920.91
million (from USD 2,671.83 million to USD 1,750.92 million).
Net returns from foreign exchange reserves management and
other foreign exchange operations accounted for MDL 2,577.75
million in 2015, increasing by about MDL 715 million compared to
2014 (MDL 1,862.38 million).
The
increase
of
return
from
foreign
exchange
reserves
management
and
other
foreign
exchange
operations
was
generated by the following factors:
• The depreciation of the MDL against the main currencies in
2015 was 25.89 percent against USD, 13.06 percent against
EUR and 20.02 percent against GBP;
• The income from the positive revaluation at the maturity
date and sale date of securities managed by the NBM and
securities managed by the World Bank under the Agreement
for investment management and consulting.
The impact of foreign exchange reserves management operations
on the ﬁnancial activity of the National Bank is presented in table
A.30.
Average proﬁtability rate of the
foreign exchange reserve
management increased by 1.34 percent in 2015 (in 2014: 1.11
percent), due to the increase in the average proﬁtability rate of
securities in foreign currency, including net income from securities
sale and the increase of the average proﬁtability rates of term
deposits (Chart 3.81).
At the same time, net result recorded in 2015 from foreign

exchange reserves management was mainly determined by the
depreciation of the national currency against the main foreign
currency, the income being mainly obtained as a result of
currency sales.
It should be mentioned that in accordance with best international
practices and the Law on the National Bank of Moldova, the
reserve assets are selected based on safety of basic amounts and
liquidity criteria. Thus, only after ensuring these two criteria, the
instruments proﬁtability in which the state foreign exchange
reserves are invested shall be taken into account.
The evolution of the ofﬁcial foreign exchange rate of the MDL
against the currencies in which the foreign exchange reserves
are held had a signiﬁcant impact on the ﬁnancial situation of the
National Bank of Moldova. Its dynamics and the average annual
values are shown in table A.31.
At the end of 2015, foreign exchange rate ﬂuctuations resulted in
unrealized income from foreign exchange rate differences in an
amount of MDL 2,258.16 million, while lower prices for state
securities in international markets led to unrealized losses from
the revaluation of investment securities in the amount of
approximately MDL (28.54) million. Unrealized income did not
affect the size of the proﬁt available for distribution, as according
to the Law on the National Bank of Moldova, unrealised income


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova in 2015
111
was transferred to the respective reserve accounts of unrealised
income.
At the end of 2014, unrealized revenue from the foreign exchange
rate differences from the revaluation of foreign currency stocks
amounted to MDL 2,372.84 million, and the unrealized revenue
from foreign exchange rate differences from the revaluation of
investment securities MDL 12.28 million, which were transferred
to the respective reserve accounts.
Capital and reserves
The activities conducted by the National Bank in 2015 to fulﬁl its
basic duties had an impact on the bank’s balance sheet structure.
This impact is shown in table A.32.
The situation of capital and reserves is reﬂected in table A.33.
On 31 December 2015, the amount of MDL 1,387.94 million
was allocated to general reserve fund and the amount of MDL
572.95 million to authorised capital, as a result of recording a
proﬁt available for distribution in the amount of MDL 1,960.89
million, which was used for statutory capital increase in a share
of 100 percent, according to the Law on the National Bank of
Moldova. Thus, on 31 December 2015, the general reserve fund
recorded a level of MDL 1,805.94 million, while authorised capital -
MDL 902.97 million, increasing respectively the statutory capital,
totalling MDL 2,708.91 million.
As at 31 December 2015, bank’s capital and reserves amounted
to
MDL
10,289.04
million,
increasing
as
compared
to
31
December 2014 amid recording unrealized revenue from foreign
exchange rate differences on revaluation of foreign currency
stocks in the amount of MDL 2,258.16 million and from the
revaluation of investment securities – MDL (28.54) million, which
modiﬁed correspondingly the respective reserve accounts.
Other reserves comprise reserve income from the revaluation
of state securities, which recorded a total negative result that
amounted to MDL (0.86) million and the positive differences from
revaluation of monetary gold amounted to MDL 4.47 million.

132
Annual Report (BNM, 2015)
Table A.22: Limits on investments and constraints imposed by the NBM in the management of investment risk process
Risks
Methods of reducing risks
Credit risk
* Investment in reliable counter parties with high credit quality as established by international rating agencies Standard&Poor’s, Moody’s and
Fitch Ratings (using average rating rated by the three agencies).
* Establishing a minimum acceptable rating of counter parties authorized to conduct transactions.
* Setting limits depending on the type, duration and rating of investment instruments and the currency composition.
* Daily monitoring of investment limits and compliance with credit limits.
* Credit risk quantiﬁcation, using default coefﬁcients determined by the Standard&Poor’s rating agency for each type of rating and maturity.
Market risk
* Daily and monthly monitoring of market risk is carried out by assessing the market price of unrealized gains/losses from the ﬂuctuation in
exchange rates and changes in securities prices.
* Market risk of the investment securities portfolio is managed and monitored based on the value at risk methodology (VaR and CVARs), which
estimates the potential economic losses based on a number of parameters and assumptions for various changes in market condition.
* Monitoring of interest rate risk and determination of price sensitivity of securities price from investment portfolios upon interest rate ﬂuctuations
is assessed by applying PV01.
* Establishment by the Council of Administration of the NBM of a normative foreign exchange structure of investment portfolio, which aims to
cover currency risk and portfolio optimization through proper correspondence of foreign currency composition of assets and liabilities and
through an acceptable investment horizon to ensure continuity of servicing current external obligations and implementing monetary and foreign
exchange policies of the State. The normative foreign currency composition is determined based on a comprehensive analysis of the currency
composition of trade in goods and services, foreign debt, currency in which the interventions are performed etc. The foreign currency
composition of reserves may deviate within +/- 10% of the normative foreign currency composition.
* Establishment of foreign currency structure of each sub-portfolio and allowable deviations by the Investment Committee of the NBM.
* Compliance with normative foreign currency composition and foreign exchange structure of each sub-portfolio.
Liquidity risk
* Ensuring liquidity through sight deposits and by investing in securities with high liquidity and low risk.
* Daily monitoring of liquidity risk is also carried out by using market benchmarks established for investment sub-portfolios indexed by type
of investment instrument and currency. Benchmarks are market indexes, used and recognized worldwide to measure the performance and
risks of investment portfolios.
* NBM investment policy allows active management of the reserves, with tools and time deviations limited to +/- 20% to the benchmark.
Source: NBM

Ensure the National Bank of 
Moldova with operational 
information, which will optimize 
the prudential regulation and 
strengthen the banking supervision 
within a macro prudential

activity, in line with modern 
principles of credit risk

solvency and macroeconomic 
stability etc.

in 2013 on the central bank's practices to

contract in 2015. The project initiation, 
assess and design phases has been 
achieved during 2015. At the end of the 
year, the project reached the 
implementation and customisation phase 
of the information solution. 
The implementation and 
final acceptance of the 
information solution; the 
conduct of tender 
procedure related to the 
infrastructure development 
for the credit risk register.

4 
Implementation 
of Basel II/III 
Agreement 
3. Develop the 
supervision 
function of the 
NBM 
The implementation of the 
provisions of the Basel II/III 
Agreement will provide a more 
flexible prudential framework for 
setting the capital requirements 
(from a simplified level to a 
sophisticated level), proportionate 
to risk profile of the banking 
institutions. At the same time, the 
Basel II/III will significantly increase 
the banking system stability, allow 
identification of the nature of 
activity of each banking institution 
and its risk profile, as well as it will 
ensure the diversification of risks 
evaluation methods. As a result, the 
implementation of the Basel II/III 
Agreement requirements will 
stimulate the transparency and 
market discipline. 
June 2013 
Following the signing of the Twinning 
contract by counter-parties and its 
approval by the EU authorities, the 
following activities have been carried out 
during 2015: the evaluation of the existing 
legal/regulatory framework related to 
the regulation of bank capital and 
identification of differences against the 
requirements of Basel II/III; the 
organisation of trainings for NBM 
employees and licensed banks to 
familiarise them and to develop the 
knowledge on the provisions of the IV 
Directive (CRD IV package) applied in the 
EU; drawing up questionnaires to assess 
the quantitative and qualitative impact on 
the domestic banking sector. At the same 
time, the strategy for the implementation 
of Basel standards through the EU 
legislation has been drawn up.  
Draw up drafts for the 
amendment of the legal / 
normative acts in the 
context of the 
implementation of Directive 
2013/36/ EU and Regulation 
575/2013; 
develop supervisory 
framework based on Pillar 1 
risk categories 
(standardized and advanced 
approach); 
implement internal systems 
and procedures for risk 
management in banks by 
evaluating current systems 
and train the 
representatives from the 
banking system; 
approval by the NBM of the 
CRD IV implementation 
strategy;  
evaluate the banks' 
practices on risk 
management and 
compliance with the

5 
Modernise the 
NBM official 
website 
6. Improve 
external 
communication 
Launch the project of 
modernization and improvement of 
the structure, content and design 
of the NBM official website, which 
is one of the measures designed to 
achieve the strategic objective of 
external communication 
improvement. The NBM official 
website aims at providing access to 
general public to accurate and 
operational information on the 
NBM activity and the situation of 
the banking system in the Republic 
of Moldova and it is considered to 
be one of the main instruments 
ensuring the communication with 
stakeholders, contributing to the 
NBM image formation outdoors. 
Based on this, the project aims to 
conduct a comprehensive review of 
the structure and content, the web 
design, interactive elements, which 
will create necessary premises for 
optimizing the NBM communication 
with external parties. 
November 
2012 
Following the selection of a service 
provider, a new NBM official website has 
been developed in 2014. A series of 
activities have been performed in this 
regard, such as: creation of an interactive 
data base, development of data access 
technologies, transfer of data from the 
archives of the old website to the new 
website, trainings of information writers 
and providers, synchronisation of old 
information content with the new 
website.  
The NBM official website has been 
modernised and lunched on 4 June 2015.   
Benefits achieved:  
Focus on the National Bank of Moldova 
objectives;  
Implementation of the interactive data 
base with dynamic statistical data;   
Presentation of data in the interactive 
infographic regime; 
Availability of the information 
prioritisation mechanism; 
Personalisation of the page content; 
Availability of the modern information 
search and filter mechanism; 
Project achieved 
successfully.   
 
Ongoing development of 
the official webpage 
functionalities.

6 
Transform NBM 
8. Increase the 
efficiency of 
the NBM 
operational 
activities 
To assess and transform the NBM 
business processes relating of 
banking operating, management of 
financial, material and human 
resources and to modernize the 
NBM banking IT system in order to 
achieve the objective of increasing 
the efficiency of the NBM 
operational activity. The aim of 
Transform NBM Project focuses on 
a multidimensional transformation 
of the organization with the 
purpose of ensuring maximum 
alignment to its strategic 
objectives. This approach is based 
on large-scale architectural model 
at the organization level 
(Enterprise Architecture), which, if 
implemented, provides correlation 
between the activity processes, 
organizational structure, staff, 
application systems, information, 
technologies etc. and the objectives 
of the organization. 
March 2012 
During 2014-2015, the projects of the 
NBM Transformation Plan have been 
implemented, after the completion of the 
project first phase in 2013, during which 
the target model of the NBM organisation 
has been defined based on the aspects of 
activity processes, human resources, 
organisational structure and information 
technologies. There have been 
implemented short and medium-term 
measures for achieving fast performances 
in the activity processes, human 
resources and organisational structure. 
Additionally, in 2015, the performance 
management system of NBM's employees 
has been implemented and the bank's 
organizational structure (II phase) has 
been adjusted, achieving synergies by 
grouping similar activities, segregation of 
functions and simplification of 
hierarchical reporting structures. At the 
same time, the NBM' activity processes 
management systems and projects have 
been developed. These performances 
contributed to the efficiency of banks 
internal resources use through the 
Announcement of 
international open tenders 
for the selection and 
implementation of certain 
modern and advanced 
information systems for 
resources and banking 
operations management.  
The ongoing 
implementation of the 
measures from the Action 
Plan to achieve 
performances on medium 
and long-term.

8. Increase the

operational

Ensure sustainable development of 
the monetary and foreign exchange 
market by strengthening trading 
processes on a unique platform, 
through a technological 
modernization of processes, by 
ensuring a high degree of 
effectiveness, efficiency and 
transparency and by strengthening

October 
2012 
During 2013-2014, the unique trading 
platform for the foreign exchange 
operations carried out on the domestic 
market has been implemented to carry 
out auctions of state securities 
placements (SS) on the primary market, 
and for some monetary market 
operations, such as: placement of the 
NBM Certificates, sale/purchase of SS 
from/in the NBM portfolio, deposits 
attraction from banks through auctions. 
At the same time, since July 2013, 
Bloomberg became the fixing agent to 
determine the CHIBOR/CHIBID reference 
rates on interbank monetary market, 
while in November 2014, the banks show 
via Bloomberg the quotations for the SS 
sale/purchase on the secondary market.  
The method of calculation and 
distribution of the SS market prices 
eligible for liquidity-providing operations 
has been implemented during 2015. At the 
same time, the collaboration with 
Finalise the platform 
elaboration by Bloomberg 
for carrying out 
repo/reverse repo 
operations and 
performance of credit 
auctions at variable rates, 
setting up its connection 
with the NBM information 
systems and modification of 
the regulatory framework.

Optimization of

8. Increase the

activities 
Automation of  
cash processing activity purchasing  
modern equipment  
with extended functions.  
January 
2015 
Following the project initiation in the first 
half of 2015, the procurement procedure 
through open tender was conducted to 
select the provider of banknote 
processing and packaging machines. 
Thereafter, the project implementation 
took place in the second half of 2015: the 
adaptation of necessary spaces to install 
the machines simultaneously with the 
equipment production by the provider, 
the equipment installation and 
adjustment and their testing and putting 
into service.    
Benefits achieved:  
Total automation of the cash 
processing/packing activity, including the 
shredding of cash unfit for circulation; 
Increase of cash processing speed and 
capacity; 
Increase of process security level; 
Improvement of working conditions; 
Creation of premises to introduce new 
standards for the cash put into circulation 
and to regulate the cash interbank 
market in terms of quality.  
Project achieved 
successfully.
